\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j};\ell\geqslant j}2^{\ell\beta}+\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j};\ell<j}2^{\ell\beta}\leqslant\sum_{\ell\leqslant\ln D/\ln 2}2^{\ell\beta}+\sum_{\ell<j}2^{\ell\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim D^{\beta}+2^{j\beta}\lesssim 2^{j\beta}(1+t2^{j\alpha})^{\beta}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Using the above two lemmas, we can show the following crucial lemma.

Lemma 2.12
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
with
{\boldsymbol{p}}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}
. Write
{\mathscr{A}}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}})
.
Recall
{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}:={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
.

(i)

(High frequency part of
P_{t}f
)
For any
\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\gamma\geqslant 0
, there is a constant
C=C(\alpha,d,{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime},\beta,\gamma)>0
such that for any
f\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
and all

j\geqslant 1
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim_{C}2^{j({\mathscr{A}}-\beta)}((2^{j\alpha}t)^{-\gamma}\wedge 1)\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

(2.25)

(ii)

(Low frequency part of
P_{t}f
)
For any
\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}
, there is a constant
C=C(\alpha,d,{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime},\beta)>0
such that for any
f\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
and all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim_{C}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

(2.26)

and for any
F\in({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})^{d}
and all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim_{C}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+1}{\alpha}}\|F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

(2.27)

Proof.

(i) By the interpolation theorem, we may assume
\beta\not=0
.
Note that by (i) of Lemma
2.11
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f={\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Big(\Gamma_{t}p_{t}*\Gamma_{t}f\Big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{SE1}}}{{=}}\sum_{\ell\geqslant 0}{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}*\Gamma_{t}{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f=\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}*\Gamma_{t}{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f.

(2.28)

By Bernstein’s inequality (
2.7
) and Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{j{\mathscr{A}}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\leqslant 2^{j{\mathscr{A}}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}\|\Gamma_{t}{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AA1}}}{{=}}2^{j{\mathscr{A}}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{j{\mathscr{A}}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}2^{-\ell\beta}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AS88}}}{{\lesssim}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}2^{j({\mathscr{A}}-\beta)}(1+(t2^{j\alpha}))^{|\beta|}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

which together with Lemma
2.8
with
l=\gamma
and
l=\gamma+|\beta|
yields (
2.25
).

(ii) We only prove (
2.27
).
Noting that by definitions,

{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F=\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}*\Gamma_{t}(p_{t}*\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F)=\Gamma_{t}(\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}*(p_{t}*\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F)),

we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AA1}}}{{=}}\|\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}*(p_{t}*\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F)\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}.

(2.29)

By Young’s inequality and integration by parts, we have
for
\varepsilon>0
,

\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\leqslant\|\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}\|_{1}\|\nabla_{v}p_{t}*F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\|\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}\|_{1}\|\nabla_{v}p_{t}*F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\varepsilon}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

which, by (
2.13
) and Lemma
2.10
, implies that for
{\boldsymbol{q}}\in[1,\infty]
being defined by
\boldsymbol{1}+\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}=\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{q}}}+\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}
and

t\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{v}p_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\varepsilon-\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{q}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim t^{\frac{{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(d/{\boldsymbol{q}}-{\boldsymbol{d}})-1}{\alpha}}\|F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}=t^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+1}{\alpha}}\|F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

(2.30)

Moreover, for
t\in(0,1]
and
\varepsilon>0
, by (
2.29
) and (
2.13
),

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\|p_{t}*(\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}*\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F)\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\leqslant\|p_{t}\|_{1}\|\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}*\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\|(\nabla_{v}\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0})*F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\|(\nabla_{v}\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0})*F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\varepsilon}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{v}\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\varepsilon-\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{q}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

Since
\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}\in{\mathcal{S}}
, by the chain rule, we have

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|\nabla_{v}\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\varepsilon-\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{q}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\sum_{j\leqslant|\varepsilon-\beta|+1}\|\nabla^{j}_{v}\Gamma_{-t}\check{\phi}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}\|_{\boldsymbol{q}}<\infty.

Hence,

\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\|F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

which together with (
2.30
) yields (
2.27
).
∎

Remark 2.13
.

The differentiability index
\beta
in (
2.26
) and (
2.27
) does not make any contribution about the decay rate of large time of
\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}
.
This is natural since we are using the non-homoegenous norm in the right hand side.

Fix
\alpha\in(0,2]
. For given
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
q\in[1,\infty]
and a Banach space
{\mathbb{B}}
,
let
{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbb{B}})
be the space of all functions
f:(0,\infty)\to{\mathbb{B}}
with finite norm

\displaystyle\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{B}})}:=\left(\int^{\infty}_{0}{\big(}\|f(t)\|_{{\mathbb{B}}}(1\wedge t)^{\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}{\big)}^{q}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t\right)^{1/q}<\infty.

If
\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1}
are positive,
then
\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma_{1}
stand for the small time singularity and large time decay weights, respectively.

We shall use the following relation for two weights
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.

Definition 2.14
.

For
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1})
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{1})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
one says
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to8.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 M 0 1.38 L 5.53 -1.38}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
if
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\geqslant\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma^{\prime}_{1}\leqslant\gamma_{1}
,
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
if
\gamma^{\prime}_{0}\geqslant\gamma_{0}
and
\gamma^{\prime}_{1}<\gamma_{1}
.
In particular, if
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to8.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-1.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 M 0 1.38 L 5.53 -1.38}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, then

\displaystyle\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{B}})}\leqslant\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{B}})}.

(2.31)

The following proposition is simple, but quite useful for proving the large time decay estimates and
the smoothness of solutions by shifting the time variable (see Lemma
3.9
below).

Proposition 2.15
.

For any
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\in[0,\infty)^{2}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|f(t+\cdot)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\gamma^{\prime}}({\mathbb{B}})}\leqslant(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{B}})},\ \ \forall t>0.

(2.32)

Proof.

Noting that for
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1}),{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{1})\in[0,\infty)^{2}
and
s,t>0
,

(1\wedge s)^{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}{\alpha}}\leqslant(1\wedge(t+s))^{\frac{\gamma_{0}+\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}

and

(1\vee s)^{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}{\alpha}}\leqslant(1\vee(t+s))^{\frac{\gamma_{0}+\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}},

by definition, we have

\displaystyle\|f(t+\cdot)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{B}})}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\|f(t+s)\|_{{\mathbb{B}}}(1\wedge s)^{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee s)^{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}{\alpha}}\Big)^{q}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/q}

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\int^{\infty}_{t}\Big(\|f(s)\|_{{\mathbb{B}}}(1\wedge s)^{\frac{\gamma_{0}+\gamma_{0}^{\prime}}{\alpha}}(1\vee s)^{\frac{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{1}^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\Big)^{q}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/q}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leqslant(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{B}})}.

The proof is complete.
∎

The following lemma provides useful estimates about the kinetic semigroup
P_{t}
.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
with
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}
. Write
{\mathscr{A}}_{i}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}})
,
i=0,1
. For any
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
, there are constants
C_{i}=C_{i}(\alpha,d,\beta,\beta^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime})>0
,
i=0,1
such that
for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbbm{1}}_{\beta^{\prime}\not=\beta-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}}\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim_{C_{0}}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{(\beta^{\prime}-\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0})\vee 0}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

(2.33)

where
{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
, and for
t\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim_{C_{1}}t^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

(2.34)

Proof.

(i) Let
\beta_{0}:=\beta^{\prime}-\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}\not=0
.
By (
2.26
) and (
2.25
) with
\gamma>\frac{\beta_{0}\vee 0}{\alpha}
, we have for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j\beta^{\prime}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j\beta_{0}}((2^{j\alpha}t)^{-\gamma}\wedge 1)\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

Since
\beta_{0}\not=0
and
\gamma\alpha>\beta_{0}\vee 0
, by Lemma
A.1
we have

\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j\beta_{0}}((2^{j\alpha}t)^{-\gamma}\wedge 1)\lesssim t^{-\frac{\beta_{0}\vee 0}{\alpha}},\ t>0.

Thus we get

\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+t^{-\frac{\beta_{0}\vee 0}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant 2(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{(\beta^{\prime}-\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0})\vee 0}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

If
\beta_{0}=0
, we directly have

\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant\sup_{j\geqslant 0}2^{j\beta^{\prime}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

Combining the above two estimates, we obtain (
2.33
).

(ii) Since
{\mathscr{A}}_{1}={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\frac{{\boldsymbol{d}}}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}})
,
by (
2.26
) and (
2.25
) with
\gamma>\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}{\alpha}
large enough, we have for
t\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j\beta^{\prime}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+t^{-\gamma}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j(\beta^{\prime}+{\mathscr{A}}_{1}-\alpha\gamma)}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 2.17
.

Estimates (
2.33
) and (
2.34
) provide short time and large time estimates of the kinetic semigroup, respectively.
In particular, the rate of large time decay only depends on the integrability indices. Note that

\|P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AB2}}}{{\lesssim}}\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AD0456}}}{{\lesssim}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0,\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AB2}}}{{\lesssim}}_{C}\|f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}},\ \ t\geqslant 1.

It is the same reason that (
2.33
) is stronger than the classical estimate:

\|\nabla_{v}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}}.

Moreover, for any
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
,
noting that by (
2.33
),

\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}\vee 0}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},\ \ t>0,

by the real interpolation theorem (see
[
8
, Theorem 6.4.5, (1)]
), we have for any
q\in[1,\infty]
,

\displaystyle\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim_{C}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}\vee 0}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},\ \ t>0.

(2.35)

For
f\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
and
t>0
, let

u(t,x,v):={\mathscr{I}}_{t}f(x,v):=\int^{t}_{0}P_{t-s}f(s,x,v){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

By the definition (
2.22
) of
P_{t}
, it is easy to see that in the distributional sense,

\partial_{t}u=\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}u-v\cdot\nabla_{x}u+f.

We now show the following basic Schauder estimate in time-weighted spaces.

Theorem 2.18
.

(Time-weighted Schauder’s estimate)
Let
\alpha\in(0,2)
,
q\in(1,\infty]
and
{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
with
{\boldsymbol{p}}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}
.
Write

{\mathscr{Q}}:=\alpha-\tfrac{\alpha}{q},\ \ {\mathscr{A}}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}).

Recall
{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}:={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
. Suppose that
\theta\in(0,{\mathscr{Q}})
and

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=(\gamma_{0},\gamma_{1}),{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}=(\gamma^{\prime}_{0},\gamma^{\prime}_{1})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
satisfy

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\in[0,{\mathscr{Q}})^{2},\ \ {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\big(\theta,({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}})\vee 0\big),

(2.36)

see Definition
2.14
for notation
.
Let
\Theta:=(\alpha,d,q,{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime},\theta)
be the parameter set and

\beta^{\prime}:={\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta.

For any
\beta\in{\mathbb{R}}
, there is a
constant
C=C(\beta,\Theta)>0
such that for any
F\in({\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}))^{d}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathscr{I}}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(2.37)

Moreover, if
\theta=0
or
{\mathscr{Q}}
, then

\displaystyle\|{\mathscr{I}}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(2.38)

Proof.

First of all, by (
2.27
), Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t-s}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F_{s}\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}(1\vee(t-s))^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+1}{\alpha}}\|F_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int^{t}_{0}\left((1\wedge(t-s)^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+1}{\alpha}})(1\wedge s)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee s)^{-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\right)^{r}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{\frac{1}{r}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{\frac{1}{r}-{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\ell^{1/r}_{\vartheta}(t)\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},

(2.39)

where
\vartheta:=r({\mathscr{A}}+1)/\alpha
and

\ell_{\vartheta}(t):=(1\vee t)^{-\vartheta\wedge 1}(1+{\mathbbm{1}}_{\vartheta=1}\ln(1\vee t)).

Noting that by (
2.36
) and
\frac{\alpha}{r}=\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{q}={\mathscr{Q}}
,

\alpha(\tfrac{1}{r}-\tfrac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}-\tfrac{{\mathscr{A}}+1}{\alpha}\wedge\tfrac{1}{r})={\mathscr{Q}}-\gamma_{1}-({\mathscr{A}}+1)\wedge{\mathscr{Q}}=({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}})\vee 0-\gamma_{1}<-\gamma^{\prime}_{1},

we have

(1\vee t)^{\frac{1}{r}-{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\ell^{1/r}_{\vartheta}(t)=(1\vee t)^{\frac{({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}})\vee 0-\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}(1+{\mathbbm{1}}_{\vartheta=1}\ln(1\vee t))^{\frac{1}{r}}\lesssim(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}{\alpha}}.

Substituting this into (
2.39
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(2.40)

Next, let
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{1}{r}=1
. By (
2.25
), Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
A.2
, we have
for any
j\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int^{t}_{0}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t-s}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F_{s}\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\lesssim 2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+1-\beta)}\int^{t}_{0}\left[((t-s)2^{j\alpha})^{-2}\wedge 1\right]\|\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+1-\beta)}\left[\int^{t}_{0}\Big([((t-s)2^{j\alpha})^{-2}\wedge 1](1\wedge s)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee s)^{-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\Big)^{r}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right]^{\frac{1}{r}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+1-\beta)}((2^{j\alpha}t)^{-\frac{1}{r}}\wedge 1)(1\wedge t)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{\frac{1}{r}-{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(2.41)

Since
\frac{\alpha}{r}={\mathscr{Q}}
, for any
\theta\in(0,{\mathscr{Q}})
, by Lemma
A.1
we get

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j(\beta+{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta)}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j({\mathscr{Q}}-\theta)}((2^{j\alpha}t)^{-\frac{1}{r}}\wedge 1)(1\wedge t)^{\frac{1}{r}-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{\frac{1}{r}-{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim t^{-\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-\theta}{\alpha}}(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\qquad=(1\wedge t)^{\frac{\theta-\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{{\frac{\theta-\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

Since
\gamma^{\prime}_{1}<\gamma_{1}
, one can choose
\theta^{\prime}\in(0,\theta]
so that
\theta^{\prime}-\gamma_{1}\leqslant-\gamma_{1}^{\prime}

and for
t\geqslant 1
,

\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j(\beta+{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta^{\prime})}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim t^{{\frac{\theta^{\prime}-\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\leqslant t^{-{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

Thus, we further have

\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j(\beta+{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta)}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{\theta-\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-{\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},

which, together with (
2.40
) and
\theta<{\mathscr{Q}}
, yields

\displaystyle\|{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}+\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j(\beta+{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta)}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{\theta-\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

Moreover, by (
2.41
) we directly have

\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}{\mathscr{I}}_{t}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}F\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\left\{\begin{aligned} &2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+1-\beta-{\mathscr{Q}})}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},\\
&2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+1-\beta)}(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-\gamma_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}}\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},\end{aligned}\right.

which together with (
2.40
) gives (
2.38
) for
\theta=0
and
\theta={\mathscr{Q}}
by definition.
∎

Remark 2.19
.

From the proof, one sees that if
{\mathscr{A}}\not={\mathscr{Q}}-1
, then
\vartheta\not=1
and we can take

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}-\big(\theta,({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}})\vee 0\big).

Remark 2.20
.

About the regularity index
\beta^{\prime}=\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{q}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta
in (
2.37
),
\alpha
is the gain from the kinetic semigroup,
and
(\frac{\alpha}{q},1,{\mathscr{A}},\theta)
are, respectively, the loss from
the time integrablility of
f
, the divergence, Sobolev’s embedding and the singularity of small time weight.

3.
Well-posedness of nonlinear kinetic Fokker-Planck equation

In this section we aim to establish the global well-posedness to the following Fokker-Planck equation with small initial value:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u=(\Delta^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}_{v}-v\cdot\nabla_{x})u-\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}((b*u)u),\ u(0)=u_{0}.

(3.1)

Firstly we introduce the definition of weak solutions to the above equation.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
u_{0}\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
and
u\in L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty);L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))
for some
p\in[1,\infty]
.
We call
u
a weak solution of (
3.1
) with initial value
u_{0}
if

(b*u)u\in L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty);{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}),

and for any
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle\langle u_{t},\varphi\rangle=\langle u_{0},\varphi\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\left[\langle u_{s},(\Delta_{v}^{\alpha/2}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\varphi\rangle+\langle(b*u_{s})u_{s},\nabla_{v}\varphi\rangle\right]{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s,

(3.2)

where
\langle u_{t},\varphi\rangle:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}u_{t}(z)\varphi(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z=\int u_{t}\varphi
.

Remark 3.2
.

For
\alpha\in(0,2)
, note that up to a constant
c_{d,\alpha}>0
(see
[
69
]
),

\displaystyle\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}u(v)=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\int_{|w|\geqslant\varepsilon}\frac{u(v+w)-u(v)}{|w|^{d+\alpha}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w=\frac{1}{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\frac{u(v+w)+u(v-w)-2u(v)}{|w|^{d+\alpha}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w.

(3.3)

From this representation, it is easy to see that for
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
,

\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}\varphi\in(L^{1}\cap L^{\infty})({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

In particular,
\int^{t}_{0}\langle u_{s},\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}\varphi\rangle{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s
is well-defined for
u\in L^{1}_{loc}([0,\infty);L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))
.
Moreover, by Duhamel’s formula, (
3.2
) is equivalent to the following integral equation

\displaystyle u_{t}=P_{t}u_{0}-\int^{t}_{0}P_{t-s}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}((b_{s}*u_{s})u_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

(3.4)

3.1.
Global well-posedness of small initial value

In this subsection we prove a basic result about the global well-posedness for (
3.1
) with small initial data
based on the contraction mapping theorem.
First of all, we introduce the main indices and their assumptions.

(H
0
)

Let
\alpha\in{(1,2]}
,
q_{b}\in(\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty]

and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0},\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0},\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
with
\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}\leqslant\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}
and
\boldsymbol{1}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}}
. Let
\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{0},\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{1}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
be defined by

\tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}}=\boldsymbol{1}
and
\tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{1}}+\tfrac{1}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}}=\boldsymbol{1}.

Let
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}:={\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}\wedge\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{1}
and

{\mathscr{Q}}:=\alpha-\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}},\ \ {\mathscr{A}}_{i}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}+\tfrac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i}}-{\boldsymbol{d}})={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}-\tfrac{d}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i}}),\ i=0,1.

Let
\beta_{0}\in(-{\mathscr{Q}},0)
and
\beta_{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Suppose that
\beta_{0}\not={\mathscr{A}}_{i}-\beta_{b},i=0,1
and

\displaystyle 0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}\leqslant{\mathscr{Q}}-1\ \mbox{ and }\ {\mathscr{A}}_{0}-2\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<{\mathscr{Q}}.

(3.5)

and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}:=(\gamma_{b0},\gamma_{b1})
,

\displaystyle\gamma_{b0}=0,\ \ \gamma_{b1}\in(({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0,({\mathscr{Q}}-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 1).

(3.6)

Remark 3.3
.

Note that
(\beta_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1})
is referred to the regularity of initial data
u_{0}
and
(q_{b},\gamma_{b},\beta_{b},\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0},\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1})

is referred to the regularity of kernel function
b
. Since
\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}\leqslant\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}
and
\frac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}\geqslant\boldsymbol{1}-\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}}=\frac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{0}}
, we have

\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}-\tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{1}}=\tfrac{1}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}\wedge\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{1}}-\tfrac{1}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{1}}\leqslant\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}-\tfrac{d}{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{0}}\Rightarrow{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathscr{A}}_{0}.

Moreover, condition (
3.5
) is equivalent to the following conditions:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}\leqslant{\mathscr{Q}}-1,\ &\beta_{0}\in(-1,0),\\
&0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<{\mathscr{Q}}+\beta_{0},\ &\beta_{0}\in(-{\mathscr{Q}},-1].\end{aligned}\right.

For simplicity of notations, in the following we shall use the following parameter set

\displaystyle\Theta:=\big(\alpha,d,\beta_{0},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0},q_{b},\gamma_{b},\beta_{b},\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0},\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}\big).

(3.7)

Recalling
{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}:={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
, with the parameters in
(H
0
)
,
we introduce three basic spaces for later use: for
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}:=\bigcap_{i=0,1}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}},\ \ {\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}:=\bigcap_{i=0,1}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}},\ \ {\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta}:=\bigcap_{i=0,1}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}.

(3.8)

Note that the dual space of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}
is given by

{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}^{\prime}={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}-\beta}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}+{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}-\beta}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}.

More precisely, for any
b\in{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}^{\prime}
, it can be written as

b=b_{0}+b_{1},\ \ b_{i}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}-\beta}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}},i=0,1.

By Lemma
2.16
, we have the following estimates about the kinetic semigroup
P_{t}
in
{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}
,
{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta}
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Under
(H
0
)
, for any
\beta\geqslant 0
, there is a constant
C=C(\beta,\Theta)>0
such that
for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{C}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}},\ \ \|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\lesssim_{C}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}},

(3.9)

and for all
t\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\lesssim_{C}t^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}},\ \ \|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\lesssim_{C}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}.

(3.10)

Moreover, we also have

\displaystyle\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\lesssim_{C}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}},\ \ \|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\lesssim_{C}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}}.

(3.11)

Proof.

For
i=0,1
, since
\beta_{0}\not=0,{\mathscr{A}}_{i}-\beta_{b}
, by (
2.33
), it is easy to see that

\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

and for any
0\leqslant\beta\not=\beta_{0}+\beta_{b}-{\mathscr{A}}_{i}
,

\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{i}+\beta-\beta_{b}-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

where the second inequality is due to
{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathscr{A}}_{0}
.
Moreover, by interpolation inequality (
2.16
), the above inequality holds for all
\beta\geqslant 0
.
Thus we get (
3.9
). Similarly, estimate (
3.10
) follows by (
2.34
) and
{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathscr{A}}_{0}
.
Estimate (
3.11
) follows by (
2.35
).
∎

Now we introduce the following bilinear form for later use:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,w)(x,v):=\int^{t}_{0}P_{t-s}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}((b_{s}*u_{s})w_{s})(x,v){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

(3.12)

Note that by the change of variables and the semigroup property of
(P_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t+s}(u,w)=P_{s}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,w)+{\mathcal{H}}^{b^{t}}_{s}(u^{t},w^{t}),\ \ s,t>0,

(3.13)

where for a function
f
,

f^{t}(s)=f(t+s).

The following lemma provides important estimates about the above bilinear form,
which are consequences of Theorem
2.18
and Lemma
2.6
.

Lemma 3.5
.

Suppose
(H
0
)
.
Let
\theta\in(0,{\mathscr{Q}})
,
{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
with
{\boldsymbol{p}}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}
and write
{\mathscr{A}}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}})
.
For any
\beta\geqslant 0
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
satisfying
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}\in[0,{\mathscr{Q}})^{2}
and

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}-(\theta,({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}})\vee 0),

there is a constant
C=C(\beta,\theta,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime},\Theta)>0
such that for all
b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
,

u\in{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}
and
w\in{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big[\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}})}{\mathbbm{1}}_{\beta=0}\\
&\qquad+\big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}})}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\big){\mathbbm{1}}_{\beta>0}\Big],\end{split}

(3.14)

where
\beta^{\prime}:={\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}-\theta
.

Proof.

For any
\beta\geqslant 0
, by Theorem
2.18
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|(b*u)w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(3.15)

Since
b=b_{0}+b_{1}\in{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime}={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}+{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
,
by (
2.12
) and (
2.13
), we have for
i=0,1
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|(b_{i}*u)w\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}&\lesssim\|(b_{i}*u)w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\leqslant\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\\
&\lesssim\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}\lesssim\|b_{i}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|u\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}.\end{split}

(3.16)

Hence,

\|(b*u)w\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}

and

\displaystyle\|(b*u)w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}})}.

(3.17)

For
\beta>0
, by (iii) and (ii) of Lemma
2.6
, we have for
i=0,1
,

\displaystyle\|(b_{i}*u)w\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}+\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\|w\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|b_{i}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\Big(\|u\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}+\|u\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|w\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\Big).

Hence,

\|(b*u)w\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime}}\Big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}}}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\|w\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\Big)

and

\displaystyle\|(b*u)w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}})}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\Big).

(3.18)

Substituting (
3.17
) and (
3.18
) into (
3.15
), we obtain (
3.14
).
∎

Remark 3.6
.

If we assume that
-1<\beta_{b}<0
and
|b_{t}(x,y)|\leqslant K_{t}(x-y)
for some
K\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
, and
b*u
is replaced by
b\circledast u=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}b(x,y)u(y){\mathord{{\rm d}}}y
, then
(
3.14
) still holds for
\beta=0
.
Indeed, for the corresponding term
b\circledast u
in (
3.16
), we can estimate it as follows:

\|b\circledast u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\leqslant\|K*|u|\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{i=0,1}\|K_{i}*|u|\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\sum_{i=0,1}\|K_{i}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\||u|\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

Since
-1<\beta_{b}<0
, by characterization (
2.11
) and
||a|-|b||\leqslant|a-b|
, it is easy to see that

\||u|\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\|u\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

Hence,

\|b\circledast u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\lesssim\|K\|_{{\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}.

Remark 3.7
.

It shall be seen below that estimate (
3.14
) for
\beta=0
is enough to show
the existence and uniqueness of a weak solution for equation (
3.4
). While,
estimate (
3.14
) for
\beta>0
shall be used to show the smoothness of solutions.

The following lemma is a direct application of Lemma
3.5
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Suppose
(H
0
)
.
Let
\beta\geqslant 0
and
\theta\in(0,{\mathscr{Q}})
. Let
b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
satisfy

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}\in[0,{\mathscr{Q}})^{2}.

(i)

Let
\beta^{\prime}:={\mathscr{Q}}-1-\theta
. For any
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}-(\theta,{\mathscr{Q}}-1),

there is a
C=C(\Theta,\beta,\theta,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime})>0
such that for all

u\in{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}
and
w\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}})}\lesssim_{C}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})}\big).

(3.19)

(ii)

Let
\beta^{\prime}:={\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}+\beta_{b}-\theta
. For any
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
with

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}-(\theta,({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0),

there is a
C=C(\Theta,\beta,\theta,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime})>0
such that for all

u\in{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}
and
w\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}})}\lesssim_{C}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})}\big).

(3.20)

Proof.

(i) For
i=0,1
, by (
3.14
) with
({\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{p}})=({\boldsymbol{p}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}})}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}})}\Big).

Thus we obtain (
3.19
) by the definition of
{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}
in (
3.8
).

(ii) For
i=0,1
, noting that by
{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathscr{A}}_{0}
,

{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1}-\theta\geqslant{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\theta=\beta^{\prime}-\beta_{b},

and

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}-(\theta,({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{i})\vee 0),

by (
3.14
) with
({\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{p}})=(\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})
, we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+\beta^{\prime}-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta+{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{i}-\theta,1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}})}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}})}\Big),

which implies (
3.20
) by the definition of
{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}
in (
3.8
).
∎

Before stating and proving our main result, under
(H
0
)
, we introduce the following time-weighted spaces where the solutions
of equation (
3.1
) shall stay: for
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}:={\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\beta}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\beta}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}),

(3.21)

where

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\beta}:=\big(\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b},({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\big),\ \ {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\beta}:={\big(}\beta-\beta_{0},0{\big)}.

(3.22)

It should be noticed that the norm
\|\cdot\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}
does not have the monotonicity with respect to
\beta
, i.e.,

\|f\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\not\leqslant C\|f\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}},\ \ \beta>0.

But the following interpolation inequality holds: for any
\theta\in[0,1]
and
\beta>0
,

\displaystyle\|f\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\theta\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|f\|^{\theta}_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}.

(3.23)

Indeed, by the interpolation inequality (
2.16
), we have
\|f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\theta\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}^{\theta}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}^{1-\theta}
and

\displaystyle\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\theta\beta}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\theta\beta})}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t>0}\left(\|f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\theta\beta}}(1\wedge t)^{\theta\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}(1\vee t)^{({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{t>0}\left(\|f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}(1\wedge t)^{\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}(1\vee t)^{({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}\right)^{\theta}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sup_{t>0}\left(\|f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}(1\wedge t)^{{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}(1\vee t)^{({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}}\right)^{1-\theta}

\displaystyle=\|f\|^{\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\beta}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|f\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}.

Similarly,

\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\theta\beta}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\theta\beta})}\lesssim\|f\|^{\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\beta}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})}\|f\|^{1-\theta}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}.

Combining the above two estimates, we get (
3.23
).

Let

\Lambda:=({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}+\beta_{b})>0.

Now we use Lemma
3.8
to show the following crucial lemma.

Lemma 3.9
.

Under
(H
0
)
, for any
\beta\geqslant 0
and
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\Lambda)
, there is a constant
C=C(\beta^{\prime},\beta,\Theta)>0
such that for all
b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
and
u,w\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}\cap{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{C}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big)\big(\|w\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}+\|w\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big)=:{\mathscr{U}},

(3.24)

where
{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}
is defined in (
3.21
).

Proof.

We divide the proof into three steps.

(
Step 1
) In this step we first show (
3.24
) for
\beta=\beta^{\prime}=0
.
Observe that

\gamma_{b1}\in(({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0,({\mathscr{Q}}-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 1)=({\mathscr{Q}}-1-({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1},{\mathscr{Q}}-({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}).

By the definition (
3.22
) and the assumption (
3.5
), it is easy to see that

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}=({\mathscr{A}}_{0}-2\beta_{0}-\beta_{b},({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}+\gamma_{b1})\in[0,{\mathscr{Q}})^{2}.

Since
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}-({\mathscr{Q}}-1,{\mathscr{Q}}-1)
,
by (i) of Lemma
3.8
with the choice of
\theta={\mathscr{Q}}-1
,
\beta=0
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0},\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}
,

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}
, we have

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})},

Moreover, for
\theta:={\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}+\beta_{b}
, since
{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}-(\theta,({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0),

by (ii) of Lemma
3.8
with the choice of the above
\theta
,
\beta=0
and

{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}
,
\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}={\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}
, we have

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}=\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}+\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}.

(3.25)

(
Step 2
) Let
\beta\geqslant 0
. For fixed
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,{\mathscr{Q}}-1)
, let

\theta={\mathscr{Q}}-1-\beta^{\prime}\in(0,{\mathscr{Q}}).

By (i) of Lemma
3.8
with the choice of the above
\theta
,
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=0
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}=(-\theta,-\ell_{01})
, we have

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b^{t}}_{\cdot}(u^{t},w^{t})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}+\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})}\Big).

Note that by (
2.32
) and (
3.22
),

\displaystyle\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\ell_{\beta 0}+\gamma_{00}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\beta}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})},

(3.26)

and

\displaystyle\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\ell_{00}+\gamma_{\beta 0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\beta}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})}.

(3.27)

Thanks to
\ell_{\beta 0}+\gamma_{00}=\ell_{00}+\gamma_{\beta 0}=\ell_{00}+\beta-\beta_{0}
, we further have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b^{t}}_{t}(u^{t},w^{t})\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-1-\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b^{t}}_{\cdot}(u^{t},w^{t})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-1-\beta^{\prime}-\ell_{00}-\beta+\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}{\mathscr{U}}\leqslant(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}+\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}{\mathscr{U}},

where
{\mathscr{U}}
is defined by (
3.24
).
On the other hand, by (
3.11
) and (
3.25
), we have

\displaystyle\|P_{t}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}+\gamma_{00}}{\alpha}}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}+\gamma_{00}}{\alpha}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\leqslant(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}+\gamma_{00}}{\alpha}}{\mathscr{U}}.

Combining the above two estimates and by (
3.13
) with
s=t
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{2t}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}+\gamma_{00}}{\alpha}}{\mathscr{U}}.

(3.28)

(
Step 3
)
Let
\beta\geqslant 0
. For fixed
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}+\beta_{b})
, let

\theta:={\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}+\beta_{b}-\beta^{\prime}\in(0,{\mathscr{Q}}).

By (ii) of Lemma
3.8
with the choice of the above
\theta
,
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=0
and
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}=(-\theta,0)
, we have

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b^{t}}_{\cdot}(u^{t},w^{t})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|w^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{Y}}_{0})}+\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta})}\Big).

By (
3.26
) and (
3.27
), we further have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b^{t}}_{t}(u^{t},w^{t})\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}+\beta_{b}-\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b^{t}}_{\cdot}(u^{t},w^{t})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{0}+\beta_{b}-\beta^{\prime}-\ell_{00}-\beta+\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}{\mathscr{U}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\ell_{00}+\beta+\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}{\mathscr{U}}.

Moreover, by (
3.11
) we have

\displaystyle\|P_{t}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}+\ell_{00}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}

\displaystyle\qquad\!\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{SH3}}}{{\lesssim}}(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}+\ell_{00}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}.

Hence, by (
3.13
) with
s=t
again,

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{2t}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta+\beta^{\prime}+\ell_{00}}{\alpha}}(1\vee t)^{-\frac{\ell_{01}}{\alpha}}{\mathscr{U}},

which together with (
3.28
) yields (
3.24
).
∎

Remark 3.10
.

In the situation of Remark
3.6
, (
3.24
) still holds for
\beta=0
.

Now we can state and prove our main result of this section.

Theorem 3.11
.

Under
(H
0
)
, there are two constants
C_{0},C_{1}>0
only depending on
\Theta
such that
for any
b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
and
u_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}\leqslant C_{0},

(3.29)

there exists a unique weak solution to Fokker-Planck equation (
3.1
) such that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\leqslant C_{1}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}.

(3.30)

Moreover, we have the following conclusions:

(i)

(Smoothness)
For any
\beta\geqslant 0
, there is a constant
C_{\beta}=C_{\beta}(\Theta,\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})})>0
such that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\leqslant C_{\beta}.

(3.31)

(ii)

(Stability)
For another
\widetilde{b}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})

and
\widetilde{u}_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
satisfying (
3.29
),
let
\widetilde{u}
be the unique solution corresponding to
\widetilde{b}
and
\widetilde{u}_{0}
.
Then for any
\beta\geqslant 0
,
there is a constant
C_{\beta}>0
only depending on
\beta,\Theta
and

\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})},\|\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}
such that

\displaystyle\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\leqslant C_{\beta}\big(\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big).

(3.32)

Proof.

We use the contraction mapping theorem to prove the existence and uniqueness of a weak solution.
First of all, by (
3.9
) and (
3.10
), we have for any
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\|P_{\cdot}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}.

(3.33)

Thus, by (
3.33
) and (
3.24
), we can define a map
{\mathcal{U}}:{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\to{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}
by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}(u)(t):=P_{t}u_{0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,u)

(3.34)

so that for some constants
C_{2},C_{3}>0
only depending on
\Theta
and for all
u\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}(u)\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\leqslant C_{2}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|^{2}_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}},

(3.35)

and for all
u,\widetilde{u}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}(u)-{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{u})\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)-{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\leqslant C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\Big)\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}.

(3.36)

Now let us choose

\lambda=4C_{2}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}/3=C_{1}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}},

and suppose

\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\leqslant 3/(16C_{3}C_{2})=:C_{0}.

Then it is easy to see that

\displaystyle C_{2}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\lambda^{2}\leqslant\lambda,\ \ 2C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\lambda\leqslant 1/2,

(3.37)

and by (
3.35
) and (
3.36
),
{\mathcal{U}}
is a contraction mapping on the closed ball

{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda}:=\Big\{u\in{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}:\|u\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\leqslant\lambda\Big\}.

Therefore, there is a unique fixed point
u\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda}
so that for all
t>0
,

\displaystyle u(t)={\mathcal{U}}(u)(t)=P_{t}u_{0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,u).

(3.38)

(
Smoothness
) In this step we show the smoothness of the solution by induction and (
3.24
).
Suppose that we have shown (
3.31
) for some
\beta\geqslant 0
.
Then by (
3.33
) and (
3.24
), we have for any
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\Lambda)
,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\leqslant\|P_{\cdot}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}+\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}\big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big)^{2}<\infty.

(
Stability
) Let
\widetilde{u}
be the unique solution corresponding to
(\widetilde{b},\widetilde{u}_{0})
.
By (
3.38
) we have

\displaystyle u(t)-\widetilde{u}(t)=P_{t}u_{0}-P_{t}\widetilde{u}_{0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,u){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+}{\mathcal{H}}^{\widetilde{b}}_{t}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u}).

By symmetry, we may assume
\|\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\leqslant\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}
.
By (
3.36
) and (
3.37
), we have

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)-{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\leqslant C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\Big)\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}},

and

\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})-{\mathcal{H}}^{\widetilde{b}}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}=\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b-\widetilde{b}}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\leqslant C_{3}\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{2}.

Thus by (
3.33
),

\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\leqslant C_{2}\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}+C_{3}\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})},

which implies that

\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\leqslant 2C_{2}\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+2C_{3}\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}.

Next we use induction and (
3.24
) to show (
3.32
) for any
\beta>0
. Suppose that we have shown (
3.32
)
for some
\beta\geqslant 0
. Then by (
3.33
) and (
3.24
), we have for any
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\Lambda)
,

\displaystyle\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|P_{\cdot}(u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}+\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)-{\mathcal{H}}^{\widetilde{b}}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)-{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}+\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b-\widetilde{b}}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}

\displaystyle+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}+\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\Big)+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}},

which, by the induction hypothesis and (
3.31
), in turn implies that

\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}.

Thus we complete the proof.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

Estimate (
3.32
) provides the continuous dependence of the solutions with respect to the kernel function
b
and
initial value
u_{0}
.
We assume
b=b_{0}+b_{1}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
,
where
b_{0}
has low integrability and
b_{1}
has high integrability, which is motivated by example (
A.5
).
Moreover, we require
\gamma_{b}>({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0
, which means that
b
has some time decay property as

t\to\infty
. This of course rules out the
time-independent

b
.
However, when considering the finite time interval, we can drop this assumption. Indeed,
for fixed
T\geqslant 1
, by definition it is easy to see that

\displaystyle\|b{\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T]}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}\leqslant T^{\frac{\gamma_{b}}{\alpha}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}.

(3.39)

Thus, we can use Theorem
3.11
to
b{\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T]}
. In the next subsection,
we shall consider the large time decay estimate under extra assumptions about the initial value
u_{0}
and integrability index
\varrho_{1}
.

Remark 3.13
.

Remark
3.6
is applicable to Theorem
3.11
except that (
3.31
) and (
3.32
) only hold for
\beta\in[0,\Lambda)

since (
3.24
) still holds for
\beta=0
under the assumptions of Remark
3.6
.

3.2.
Large time decay estimates

In this subsection we drop the condition
\gamma_{b}>({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0
by assuming extra assumptions about the initial value
and integrability index
\varrho_{1}
.
More precisely, we have

Theorem 3.14
.

In addition
(H
0
)
, we assume
{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\frac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}}>{\mathscr{Q}}-1
,
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}\not=\boldsymbol{1}
and
b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
.
For any
u_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
,
there are two constants
\delta\geqslant 1
and
C_{0}>0
only depending on
\Theta
such that if

\displaystyle\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\Big(\big(\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\big)\vee 1\Big)^{\delta}\leqslant C_{0},

(3.40)

then there is a unique global weak solution
u
to FPE (
3.1
) such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left((1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\big(\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}}+\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\big)+(1\wedge t)^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\right)<\infty.

(3.41)

Moreover, we have the following conclusions:

(i)

(Smoothness)
For any
\beta\geqslant 0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left((1\wedge t)^{\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\big(\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}+\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\big)+(1\wedge t)^{\frac{\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\right)<\infty.

(3.42)

(ii)

(Stability)
For another
\widetilde{b}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{u}_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
satisfying the same condition (
3.40
),
let
\widetilde{u}
be the unique solution corresponding to
\widetilde{b}
and
\widetilde{u}_{0}
. For any
\beta\geqslant 0
,
there is a constant
C_{\beta}>0
only depending on
\Theta,\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})},\|\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}

such that
