\displaystyle\sup_{t>0}\left((1\wedge t)^{\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)-\widetilde{u}(t)\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\right)\leqslant C_{\beta}\big(\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big).

(3.43)

(iii)

(Large time decay estimate)
For any
\beta\geqslant 0
and
i=0,1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\left(t^{\frac{{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot({\boldsymbol{d}}-d/{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)<\infty.

(3.44)

Proof.

The proof is similar to Theorem
3.11
. But we need to carefully treat the large time decay estimates.
Since
{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\frac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}}>{\mathscr{Q}}-1
and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}\not=1
,

{\mathscr{Q}}-1-({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}=({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0<{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}}-{\mathscr{A}}_{1}={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot({\boldsymbol{d}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}}),

one can fix two large time decay weights
\gamma_{1},\ell_{1}\in[0,\infty)
with
\gamma_{1}+\ell_{1}\in[0,{\mathscr{Q}})
and

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}\in(({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0,{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot({\boldsymbol{d}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}})),\ \ \ell_{1}\in({\mathscr{Q}}-1,{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}}).

(3.45)

Let the weights
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\beta}
and
{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\beta}
in the definition (
3.21
) of solution space
{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}
be replaced by

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\beta}:={\big(}\beta-\beta_{0},\gamma_{1}{\big)},\ \ {\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\beta}:=\big(\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b},\ell_{1}\big).

(3.46)

In the following we divide the proof into three steps.

(
Step 1
) In this step we show that for any
\beta\geqslant 0
,
there are constants
C_{1},C_{2},\delta_{0},\delta_{1}>0
only depending on
\Theta
such that for any
T\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|P_{\cdot}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\leqslant C_{1}T^{\delta_{0}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+C_{2}T^{-\delta_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

(3.47)

As in proving (
3.10
), by (
2.34
) and
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\left(t^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{t\in[1,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\right)+\sup_{t\geqslant T}\left(t^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\sup_{t\geqslant T}\left(t^{\frac{\gamma_{1}-{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot({\boldsymbol{d}}-d/{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1})}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant T^{\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+T^{\frac{\gamma_{1}-{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot({\boldsymbol{d}}-d/{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1})}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

and by
\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{1}\leqslant\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\left(t^{\frac{\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{t\in[1,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\right)+\sup_{t\geqslant T}\left(t^{\frac{\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\sup_{t\geqslant T}\left(t^{\frac{\ell_{1}-{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot d/\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant T^{\frac{\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+T^{\frac{\ell_{1}-{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot d/\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

Moreover, by (
3.9
), we also have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,1]}\left(t^{\frac{\gamma_{\beta 0}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}+t^{\frac{\ell_{\beta 0}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\right)\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}.

Combining the above estimates and by definition (
3.21
), we obtain (
3.47
).

(
Step 2
) In this step we show the existence and uniqueness of a global solution under some smallness condition (
3.40
).
With the weights (
3.46
), by Lemma
3.8
, it is completely the same as Lemma
3.9
to derive
that for any
\beta\geqslant 0
and
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\Lambda)
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta+\beta^{\prime}}}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}+\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big)\big(\|w\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}+\|w\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\big).

(3.48)

Thus by (
3.47
) and (
3.48
),
we can define a map
{\mathcal{U}}:{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}\to{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}
by

{\mathcal{U}}(u)(t):=P_{t}u_{0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,u)

so that for some constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
only depending on
\Theta
and for all
u\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
,

\|{\mathcal{U}}(u)\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\leqslant C_{1}T^{\delta_{0}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+C_{2}T^{-\delta_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|^{2}_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}},

and for all
u,\widetilde{u}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
,

\|{\mathcal{U}}(u)-{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{u})\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}=\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)-{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(\widetilde{u},\widetilde{u})\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\leqslant C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big(\|u\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\big)\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}.

Now let us choose
T\geqslant 1
large enough and be fixed so that

C_{2}T^{-\delta_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant 1/(12C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}).

Suppose that for
\delta=\delta_{0}/\delta_{1}
and some
C_{0}=C_{0}(\Theta)>0
,

\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\leqslant\tfrac{T^{-\delta_{0}}}{12C_{1}C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}}\leqslant\tfrac{C_{0}}{\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}((\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{1;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\vee 1)^{\delta}},

where we have used
T\geqslant 1
in the second inequality.
It is easy to see that for
\lambda=\tfrac{1}{4C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}}
,

C_{1}T^{\delta_{0}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+C_{2}T^{-\delta_{1}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+C_{3}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\lambda^{2}\leqslant\lambda.

In particular,

\displaystyle\mbox{${\mathcal{U}}$ is a contraction mapping on }{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda}:=\big\{u\in{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}:\|u\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\leqslant\lambda\big\}.

(3.49)

On the other hand, letting

{\boldsymbol{p}}={\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}=\boldsymbol{1},\ \ {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}=(-\beta_{0},0),\ \ \theta={\mathscr{Q}}-1,\ \ \bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0},

by (
3.14
) with the above choice of parameters, we also have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,w)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|w\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(3.50)

Moreover, by (
2.33
) and (
2.34
),

\|P_{\cdot}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

Hence, there are two constants
C_{4},C_{5}>0

such that for all
u,\widetilde{u}\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}(u)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\leqslant C_{4}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+C_{5}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},

(3.51)

and

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}(u)-{\mathcal{U}}(\widetilde{u})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\leqslant C_{5}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big(\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}}\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\big).

(3.52)

Without loss of generality, we may assume
C_{5}\leqslant C_{3}
, where
C_{3}
is the same as above.
Otherwise, we may replace above
C_{3}
by bigger
C_{5}
.
Thus by (
3.49
), (
3.51
) and (
3.52
), it is standard to derive that there is a unique
u\in{\mathbb{S}}_{0}\cap{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})
such that

u(t)={\mathcal{U}}(u)(t)=P_{t}u_{0}{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,u)

and

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}({\mathbf{B}}^{0,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

(3.53)

Moreover, the proofs of smoothness and stability are completely the same as Theorem
3.11
.

(
Step 3
) In this step we prove the optimal large time decay estimate (
3.44
)
by shifting time variable as used in Lemma
3.9
. Let

\eta_{i}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\big({\boldsymbol{d}}-\tfrac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}}\big),\ i=0,1.

Note that by (
3.4
) and (
3.13
),

u(t+s)=P_{s}u(t){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-}{\mathcal{H}}_{s}^{b^{t}}(u^{t},u^{t}),\ \ s,t>0,

where
u^{t}(s):=u(t+s)
. Thus for fixed
\beta\geqslant 0
and any
t\geqslant 1
and
i=0,1
,

\displaystyle\|u(2t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant\|P_{t}u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\|{\mathcal{H}}_{t}^{b^{t}}(u^{t},u^{t})\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

For the first term, by (
2.34
) and (
3.53
) we have

\|P_{t}u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{0}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},\ \ t\geqslant 1.

For the second term, by (
3.14
) with
\theta={\mathscr{Q}}-1
and

\bar{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}={\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}=0,\ \ {\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}=(-\theta,\gamma^{\prime}_{1}),\ \gamma^{\prime}_{1}\in(-\ell_{1},1-{\mathscr{Q}}),

we derive that for any
t\geqslant 1
and
i=0,1
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\cdot}^{b^{t}}(u^{t},u^{t})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|b^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Sh1}}}{{\lesssim}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}t^{-\frac{\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{\beta}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\beta})}\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

Combining the above two estimates, we obtain that for any
t\geqslant 1
,

\|u(2t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim t^{-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+t^{-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u^{t}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},\ i=0,1.

In particular, there are two constants
C_{6}=C_{6}(\beta,\Theta)>0
and
C_{7}=C_{7}(\beta,\Theta,\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})})>0

such that for any
t\geqslant 2
,

\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant C_{6}t^{-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+C_{7}t^{-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\|u^{t/2}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})},\ i=0,1.

Since
\gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ell_{1}>0
, one can choose
T_{0}=T_{0}(\beta,\Theta,\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})},\gamma_{1}^{\prime},\ell_{1})\geqslant 2
large enough so that

C_{7}T_{0}^{-\frac{\gamma^{\prime}_{1}+\ell_{1}}{\alpha}}\leqslant 2^{-1-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}},\ \ i=0,1,

and so,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geqslant T_{0}}\left(t^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{6}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+2^{-1-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\sup_{t\geqslant T_{0}}\left(t^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u^{t/2}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\right)

\displaystyle=C_{6}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+2^{-1-\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\sup_{t\geqslant T_{0}}\left(t^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\sup_{s\geqslant t/2}\|u(s)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{6}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\frac{1}{2}\sup_{t\geqslant T_{0}}\sup_{s\geqslant t/2}\left(s^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u(s)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=C_{6}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\frac{T_{0}^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}}{2}\sup_{s\in[\frac{T_{0}}{2},T_{0}]}\|u(s)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\frac{1}{2}\sup_{s\geqslant T_{0}}\left(s^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u(s)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right).

Hence,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geqslant T_{0}}\left(t^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2C_{6}\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+T_{0}^{\frac{\eta_{i}}{\alpha}}\sup_{s\in[\frac{T_{0}}{2},T_{0}]}\|u(s)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant C_{8}.

The large time decay estimate now follows.
∎

3.3.
Local well-posedness of large initial value

In this section we study the local well-posedness for FPE (
3.1
) with large initial values.
First of all we show the following asymptotic estimate of short time for
P_{t}f
in Besov spaces.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
\beta,\beta^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
{\boldsymbol{p}},{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
with
{\boldsymbol{p}}\leqslant{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}
.
Let
{\mathscr{A}}:={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot(\frac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}}-\frac{d}{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}})
. Suppose that

\beta<0,\ \ {\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta>0.

For any
f\in\cup_{q\in[1,\infty)}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
, it holds that

\lim_{t\downarrow 0}\left(t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta^{\prime},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)=0

Proof.

By the Bernstein inequality (
2.7
) and (
2.28
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim 2^{j{\mathscr{A}}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}}\leqslant 2^{j{\mathscr{A}}}\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}*\Gamma_{t}{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}}.

Here and below the implicit constant does not depend on
t\in(0,1)
.
Thus by Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j\beta^{\prime}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime})}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}}=\sum_{\ell\geqslant 0}c_{\ell,t}2^{\ell\beta}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}},

where

c_{\ell,t}:=t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime})}2^{-\ell\beta}{\mathbbm{1}}_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}.

By the definition of
\Theta^{t}_{j}
, Lemmas
2.8
and
A.1
, for
l\geqslant\frac{2({\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}{\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle c_{\ell,t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geqslant 1}(t2^{j\alpha})^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}(t2^{\ell\alpha})^{-\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}{\mathbbm{1}}_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\geqslant 1}(t2^{j\alpha})^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}(t2^{j\alpha}(1+t2^{j\alpha}))^{-\frac{\beta}{\alpha}}((t2^{j\alpha})^{-l}\wedge 1)\lesssim 1,

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\geqslant 1}c_{\ell,t}

\displaystyle=t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime})}\sum_{\ell\in\Theta^{t}_{j}}2^{-\ell\beta}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}\Gamma_{t}p_{t}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\!\!\!\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AS88}}}{{\lesssim}}t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j({\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}(1+(t2^{j\alpha}))^{-\beta}((t2^{j\alpha})^{-l}\wedge 1)\lesssim 1.

Hence, for any
q\in[1,\infty)
, by Hölder’s inequality,

t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j\beta^{\prime}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\left[\sum_{\ell\geqslant 0}c_{\ell,t}(2^{\ell\beta}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}})^{q}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}\left[\sum_{\ell\geqslant 1}c_{\ell,t}\right]^{1-\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\left[\sum_{\ell\geqslant 0}c_{\ell,t}(2^{\ell\beta}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}})^{q}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}.

In particular, if
f\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
, then by the dominated convergence theorem,

\displaystyle\lim_{t\downarrow 0}\left(t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}+\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{\alpha}}\sum_{j\geqslant 1}2^{j\beta^{\prime}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\right)\lesssim\left[\sum_{\ell\geqslant 0}\lim_{t\downarrow 0}c_{\ell,t}(2^{\ell\beta}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{\ell}f\|_{\boldsymbol{p}})^{q}\right]^{\frac{1}{q}}=0.

(3.54)

On the other hand, by (
2.26
), for
t\in(0,1)
,

\|{\mathcal{R}}^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{0}P_{t}f\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}}\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{p}};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

which together with (
3.54
) yields the desired limit.
∎

The following lemma is a direct application of the above lemma.

Lemma 3.16
.

Under
(H
0
)
, for any
f\in\cup_{q\in[1,\infty)}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\lim_{t\downarrow 0}\left(t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}+t^{-\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}}\right)=0.

(3.55)

Now we can show the following local well-posedness for FPE (
3.1
) with large initial value.

Theorem 3.17
.

Suppose that
(H
0
)
and one of the following two conditions hold:

(i)

b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
for some
q_{b}\in[1,\infty)
and
u_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
;

(ii)

b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
and
u_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
, where

\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}:=\cup_{q\in[1,\infty)}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}.

Then there is a small time
T_{0}>0
depending only on
\Theta
and the corresponding norms of
b
and
u_{0}

so that (
3.1
) admits a unique smooth solution
u
on
(0,T_{0}]
and for any
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\|u{\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T_{0}]}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}<\infty,

where
{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}
is defined in (
3.21
).
Moreover, for another
(\widetilde{b},\widetilde{u}_{0})
satisfying the above (i) or (ii),
let
\widetilde{u}
be the unique local solution corresponding to
(\widetilde{b},\widetilde{u}_{0})
on the time interval
[0,\widetilde{T}_{0}]
.
Then for any
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\|(u-\widetilde{u}){\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T_{0}\wedge\widetilde{T}_{0}]}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\leqslant C_{\beta}\big(\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big),

(3.56)

where
C_{\beta}=C_{\beta}\big(T_{0},\widetilde{T}_{0},\Theta,\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})},\|\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}\big)>0
.

Proof.

(i) Since for
q_{b}\in[1,\infty)
,

\lim_{T\downarrow 0}\|b{\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T]}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}=0,

for any
u_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
, one can choose
T_{0}
small enough so that
for the constant
C_{0}
in Theorem
3.11
,

\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\leqslant C_{0}/\|b{\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T_{0}]}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}.

Thus, we conclude the proof by Theorem
3.11
.

(ii) Suppose
u_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
. By the definition of
{\mathbb{S}}_{0}
and (
3.55
), we have

\lim_{T\downarrow 0}\|{\mathbbm{1}}_{(0,T]}(P_{\cdot}u_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}=\lim_{T\downarrow 0}\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\left(t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}+t^{-\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|P_{t}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}}\right)=0.

From the proof of Theorem
3.11
, one can choose
T_{0}
small enough so that

{\mathcal{U}}
defined by (
3.34
) is a contraction mapping when we limit on the time interval
[0,T_{0}]
.
Thus we can repeat the proof of Theorem
3.11
to conclude the proof.
∎

Remark 3.18
.

If the condition (
3.5
) is replaced with

0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<{\mathscr{Q}}-1\ \mbox{ and }\ {\mathscr{A}}_{0}-2\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<{\mathscr{Q}},

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle 0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<{\mathscr{Q}}+\beta_{0}\wedge(-1),

(3.57)

then the cases (i) and (ii) in Theorem
3.17
can be combined with only one case that

b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
for some
q_{b}\in[1,\infty]
and
u_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
since

{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}\subset\cap_{\varepsilon>0}\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}+\varepsilon}
. We would like to point out that the above (i) and (ii)
allow us to consider some critical cases.

3.4.
Global well-posedness of large initial value

In this subsection we shall consider two cases that the smallness condition (
3.29
) in Theorem
3.11
can be dropped.

Our first result of global well-posedness with large initial value is

Theorem 3.19
.

Let
\alpha\in{(1,2]}
,
q_{b}\in(\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty]

and
\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0},\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}\in[1,\infty]^{2}
with
\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}\leqslant\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}
.
Let
\beta_{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
\beta_{0}\in(\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}}-\alpha,0)
. Suppose that
\beta_{b}\not={\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i}}-\beta_{0}
,
i=0,1
and

\displaystyle 0<{\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}\leqslant\alpha-\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}}-1,\ \ {\boldsymbol{a}}\cdot\tfrac{d}{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0}}-2\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<\alpha-\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}},

(3.58)

and one of the following two conditions holds:

(i)

b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
for some
q_{b}\in[1,\infty)
and
u_{0}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},\infty}_{1;{\boldsymbol{a}}}
;

(ii)

b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
and
u_{0}\in\cup_{q\in[1,\infty)}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{1;{\boldsymbol{a}}}\subset{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},\infty}_{1;{\boldsymbol{a}}}.

Then there is a unique smooth solution
u
to (
3.1
) on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
so that for any
T,\beta>0
,

\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\left(t^{(\beta-\beta_{0})/\alpha}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)<\infty.

Moreover, for another
(\widetilde{b},\widetilde{u}_{0})
satisfying the above (i) or (ii),
let
\widetilde{u}
be the unique solution corresponding to
(\widetilde{b},\widetilde{u}_{0})
. For any
T,\beta>0
, there is a constant

C_{\beta}=C_{\beta}(T,\Theta,\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})},\|\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})})>0
such that

\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\left(t^{(\beta-\beta_{0})/\alpha}\|u(t)-\widetilde{u}(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\right)\leqslant C_{\beta}\big(\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big).

Proof.

If we take
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}=1
in
(H
0
)
, then (
3.5
) reduces to (
3.58
). By Theorem
3.17
,
let
T_{0}>0
be the short time of the existence of a smooth solution. For any fixed
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0}]
, we have

u\in\cap_{\beta>0}C([t_{0},T_{0}];{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})

and

\partial_{t}u=\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}u-v\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}((b*u)u),\ t\in[t_{0},T_{0}].

Now, starting from the time
t_{0}
, since
\beta_{0}<0
and
\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant C\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}
for any
q\in[1,\infty]
,
by Theorem
3.17
again, there is a time length
\delta>0
only depending on
\Theta
,
\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}

and the bound of
\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}
so that
there is a unique smooth solution on the time interval
[t_{0},t_{0}+\delta]
.
On the other hand, by Lemma
C.1
in appendix we have

\|u(t_{0}+\delta/2)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\leqslant\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}.

Next starting from the time
t_{0}+\delta/2
, we can proceed to find a unique smooth
solution to (
3.1
) on the time interval
[t_{0}+\delta/2,t_{0}+3\delta/2]
and so on. Thus we obtain a global solution.
The regularity and stability estimates are direct consequences of Theorem
3.17
.
∎

Our second global well-posedness result of large initial value is

Theorem 3.20
.

Suppose that
(H
0
)
holds with
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}=(p_{0},p_{0})
and
\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}=(\varrho_{1},\varrho_{1})
,
and one of the following two conditions holds:

(i)

b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
for some
q_{b}\in[1,\infty)
,
\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}b=0
and
u_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
;

(ii)

b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})
,
\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}b=0
and
u_{0}\in\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
, where

\widetilde{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}:=\cup_{q\in[1,\infty)}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}})\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}.

Then there is a unique smooth solution
u
to (
3.1
) on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
so that for any
T,\beta>0
,

\|u{\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T]}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}<\infty,

where
{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}
is defined by (
3.21
).
Moreover, for another
(\widetilde{b},\widetilde{u}_{0})
satisfying the above (i) or (ii),
let
\widetilde{u}
be the unique solution on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
corresponding to
(\widetilde{b},\widetilde{u}_{0})
.
Then for any
T,\beta>0
, there is a constant
C_{\beta}=C_{\beta}(T,\Theta,\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})},\|\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})})>0
such that

\|(u-\widetilde{u}){\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T]}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\leqslant C_{\beta}\big(\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big).

Proof.

Let
T_{0}>0
be the short time of the existence of a smooth solution in Theorem
3.17
.
For any fixed
t_{0}\in(0,T_{0})
, we have

u\in C([t_{0},T_{0}];C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})),\ \ {H}:=b*u\in L^{1}([t_{0},T_{0}];C^{1}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})),

and

\partial_{t}u=\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}u-v\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}({H}u),\ t\in[t_{0},T_{0}].

Since
\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}b=0
, we have
\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}{H}=0
. Thus by Lemma
C.1
in appendix, for
t\in(t_{0},T_{0})
,

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\leqslant\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}},\ \ \|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}\leqslant\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}.

(3.59)

Noting that for
p\in[1,\infty]
,

\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{p}}\leqslant\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{1}{p}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}^{\frac{1}{p}},

by
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}=(p_{0},p_{0})
and
\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1}=(\varrho_{1},\varrho_{1})
, recalling
p_{1}=p_{0}\wedge\frac{\varrho_{1}}{\varrho_{1}-1}
, we have
for any
q\in[1,\infty]
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0,1}\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AB2}}}{{\lesssim}}\sum_{i=0,1}\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{p_{i}}}\leqslant\sum_{i=0,1}\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}}^{1-\frac{1}{p_{i}}}\|u(t_{0})\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}^{\frac{1}{p_{i}}}=:\flat(t_{0}).

Now, starting from
t_{0}
, by Theorem
3.17
again, there is a time length
\delta>0
depending only on
\Theta
,

\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}
and the bound of
\flat(t_{0})
so that
there is a unique solution on the time interval
[t_{0},t_{0}+\delta]
.
On the other hand, by (
3.59
) we have

\flat(t_{0}+\delta/2)\leqslant\flat(t_{0}).

Next starting from the time
t_{0}+\delta/2
, we can proceed to find a unique smooth solution to (
3.1
) on the time interval
[t_{0}+\delta/2,t_{0}+3\delta/2]
and so on. Thus we obtain a global solution. The regularity and stability estimates are direct consequences of Theorem
3.17
.
∎

Remark 3.21
.

If
u
is probability measure, then the condition
\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}b=0
can be replaced by
(\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}b)^{-}=0

since
(\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}{H})^{-}=(\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}b)^{-}*u=0
, and by Lemma
C.1
we still have (
3.59
).

As a special case, we also consider the non-degenerate fractional Fokker-Planck equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u=\Delta^{\alpha/2}u-\mathord{{\rm div}}((b*u)u),\ \ u(0)=u_{0}.

(3.60)

By Duhamel’s formula, we have

u(t)=P_{t}u_{0}-\int^{t}_{0}P_{t-s}\mathord{{\rm div}}((b*u)u){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s=P_{t}u_{0}-{\mathcal{H}}^{b}_{t}(u,u),

where
P_{t}
is the semigroup associated with
\Delta^{\alpha/2}
.
We assume

(
\widetilde{\bf H}_{0}
)

Let
\alpha\in{(1,2]}
,
q_{b}\in(\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty]

and
p_{0},\varrho_{0},\varrho_{1}\in[1,\infty]
with
\varrho_{0}\leqslant\varrho_{1}
and
1\leqslant\tfrac{1}{p_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{\varrho_{0}}
.
Let
\bar{\varrho}_{0},\bar{\varrho}_{1}\in[1,\infty]
be defined by
\tfrac{1}{\bar{\varrho}_{0}}+\tfrac{1}{\varrho_{0}}=1
and

\tfrac{1}{\bar{\varrho}_{1}}+\tfrac{1}{\varrho_{1}}=1.

Let
p_{1}:=p_{0}\wedge\bar{\varrho}_{1}
and

{\mathscr{Q}}:=\alpha-\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}},\ \ {\mathscr{A}}_{i}:=\tfrac{d}{p_{i}}+\tfrac{d}{\varrho_{i}}-d=\tfrac{d}{p_{i}}-\tfrac{d}{\bar{\varrho}_{i}},\ i=0,1.

Let
\beta_{0}\in(-{\mathscr{Q}},0)
and
{\mathbb{R}}\ni\beta_{b}\not={\mathscr{A}}_{i}-\beta_{0},i=0,1
. Suppose that

\displaystyle 0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}\leqslant{\mathscr{Q}}-1\ \mbox{ and }\ {\mathscr{A}}_{0}-2\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<{\mathscr{Q}}.\

(3.61)

As in (
3.8
), we introduce three isotropic Besov spaces: for
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}:=\bigcap_{i=0,1}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\varrho}_{i}},\ \ \bar{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}:=\bigcap_{i=0,1}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{p_{i}},\ \ \bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta}:=\bigcap_{i=0,1}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{p_{i}}.

(3.62)

The following result is a combination of Theorems
3.11
,
3.14
,
3.19
and
3.20
.
Since it can be considered as a special case and the proofs are completely the same, we omit the details.

Theorem 3.22
.

Suppose
(
\widetilde{\bf H}_{0}
)
and
b\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
, where
\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime}={\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\varrho_{0}}+{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\varrho_{1}}
is the dual space of
\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{0}
.
For any
\gamma_{b}>({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0
, there is a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\Theta)>0

such that for any fixed
T\geqslant 1
and
u_{0}\in\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
satisyfying

\displaystyle\|u_{0}\|_{\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\leqslant C_{0}T^{-\gamma_{b}/\alpha},

(3.63)

there is a unique weak solution
u
to FPE (
3.60
) on
[0,T]
such that

\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\left(t^{-\frac{\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{\bar{\mathbb{Y}}_{0}}+t^{\frac{{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{0}}\right)<\infty.

Moreover, we have the following conclusions:

(i)

(Smoothness)
For any
\beta\geqslant 0
, it holds that

\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{\bar{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}+t^{\frac{\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}\right)<\infty.

(ii)

(Stability)
For another
\widetilde{b}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})
and
\widetilde{u}_{0}\in\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
satisfying the same condition (
3.63
),
let
\widetilde{u}
be the unique solution on
[0,T]
corresponding to
\widetilde{b}
and
\widetilde{u}_{0}
. For any
\beta\geqslant 0
,
there is a constant
C_{\beta}=C_{\beta}(T,\Theta,\|b\|_{\bar{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})},\|\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})})>0
such that

\sup_{t\in(0,T]}\left(t^{\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)-\widetilde{u}(t)\|_{\bar{\mathbb{Y}}_{\beta}}\right)\leqslant C_{\beta}\big(\|u_{0}-\widetilde{u}_{0}\|_{\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}(\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\big).

(iii)

(Large time decay estimate)
If
\frac{d}{\varrho_{1}}>{\mathscr{Q}}-1
,
p_{0}\not=1
and
u_{0}\in\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{1}
,
then we can take
\gamma_{b}=0
in (
3.63
), and the constant
C_{0}
may depend on
\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{1}}
, and there is a global solution on
{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
so that
for any
\beta\geqslant 0
and
i=0,1
,

\sup_{t\geqslant 1}\left(t^{\frac{d-d/p_{i}}{\alpha}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{p_{i}}}\right)<\infty.

(iv)

(Global solution)
In addition, we
suppose that one of the following four conditions holds:

(a)

q_{b}\in[1,\infty)
and
u_{0}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{1}
.

(b)

q_{b}\in[1,\infty)
,
\mathord{{\rm div}}b=0
and
u_{0}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{p_{0}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{p_{1}}
.

(c)

q_{b}=\infty
and
u_{0}\in\cup_{q\in[1,\infty)}{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{1}
.

(d)

q_{b}=\infty
,
\mathord{{\rm div}}b=0
and
u_{0}\in\cup_{q\in[1,\infty)}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{p_{0}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},q}_{p_{1}})
.

Then we can drop the smallness assumption (
3.63
) and have a global solution.

4.
Well-posedness of mean-field kinetic SDEs

In this section we use previous results to show the weak and strong well-posedness for mean-field kinetic SDEs.
Although the associated nonlinear Fokker-Planck equation has a smooth solution for any positive time, we have to carefully deal with
the time singularity at initial time.

4.1.
Well-posedness of linear kinetic SDEs

In this subsection, we first study the weak/strong well-posedness to the following linear kinetic SDE:
for
\alpha\in(1,2]
,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &X_{t}=X_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}V_{s}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s,\\
&V_{t}=V_{0}+\int^{t}_{0}{H}_{s}(X_{s},V_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+L^{(\alpha)}_{t},\end{aligned}\right.

(4.1)

where
{H}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
is a measurable vector field, and
(L^{(\alpha)}_{t})_{t\geqslant 0}
is the
\alpha
-stable process as in the introduction.
Moreover, if we let
Z_{t}:=(X_{t},V_{t})
,
{\mathbf{H}}_{t}(z)={\mathbf{H}}_{t}(x,v)=(v,{H}_{t}(x,v))^{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
and
\sigma:=(0,{\mathbb{I}})^{*}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\otimes{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, then SDE (
4.1
) can be written as

\displaystyle Z_{t}=Z_{0}+\int^{t}_{0}{\mathbf{H}}_{s}(Z_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\sigma L^{(\alpha)}_{t},\ Z_{0}\sim\mu_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

(4.2)

For
q\in[1,\infty]
,
T>0
and a Banach space
{\mathbb{B}}
, we write

{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbb{B}}):=L^{q}([0,T];{\mathbb{B}}),\ {\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}:=L^{\infty}([0,T]\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

We have the following well-posedness result for SDE (
4.2
).

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\alpha\in(1,2]
,
q\in(\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty]
and
T,\beta>0
.

(i)

(Weak well-posedness)
Suppose
{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})
. For any initial distribution
\mu_{0}
,
there is a unique weak solution
Z
to kinetic SDE (
4.2
).

(ii)

(Strong well-posedness)
Suppose
{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{1+\alpha}}_{x}\cap{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})
, where
\beta>1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}
. For any initial value
Z_{0}
,
there is a unique strong solution
Z
to kinetic SDE (
4.2
).

(iii)

(Strong stability)
Let
\widetilde{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{1+\alpha}}_{x}\cap{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})
be another vector field with the same
\beta>1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{0}
another initial random variable
on the same probability space. Let
\widetilde{Z}
be the strong solution of (
4.2
) corresponding to
\widetilde{H}
and
\widetilde{Z}_{0}
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z_{t}-\widetilde{Z}_{t}|^{2}\right)\lesssim_{C}{\mathbb{E}}|Z_{0}-\widetilde{Z}_{0}|^{2}+\|H-\widetilde{H}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.3)

To show this result, we need to study the following linear kinetic equation with transport drift:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u=\Delta_{v}^{\alpha/2}u+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u-\lambda u+{H}\cdot\nabla_{v}u+f,\quad u_{0}=0,

(4.4)

where
\lambda>0
,
{H}:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
f:{\mathbb{R}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
are two Borel measurable functions.

We first show the following simple result.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
T>0
,
\alpha\in(1,2]
,
\beta,\gamma>0
and
q\in(\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty]
.

(i)

For any
{H},f\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})
,
there is a unique weak solution
u
to equation (
4.4
) with the regularity that for any
q^{\prime}\in[q,\infty]
,
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\alpha+\frac{\alpha}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\alpha}{q})

and all
\lambda\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q^{\prime}}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\lesssim_{C}\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})},

(4.5)

where
C>0
only depends on
T,d,\beta,\alpha,q,q^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}
and
\|{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}
.

(ii)

Let
{H},f,\widetilde{H},\widetilde{f}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})
and
u,\widetilde{u}
be the solutions of
equation (
4.4
) corresponding to
({H},f)
and
(\widetilde{H},\widetilde{f})
, respectively.
For any
q^{\prime}\in[q,\infty]
and
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\alpha+\frac{\alpha}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\alpha}{q})
,
there is a constant
C>0
depending on
T,d,\beta,\alpha,q,q^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}
and
\|{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}
,

\|\widetilde{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}
such that for all
\lambda\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|u-\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q^{\prime}}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\Big(\|H-\widetilde{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|f-\widetilde{f}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\Big).

(4.6)

(iii)

Let
{H},f\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})
and
u
be the solution of (
4.4
).
For any
q^{\prime}\in[q,\infty]
and
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\alpha+\frac{\alpha}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\alpha}{q})
,
there is a constant
C>0
depending on
T,d,\gamma,\beta,\alpha,q,q^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}
and
\|{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})}
such that for all
\lambda\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q^{\prime}}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})},

(4.7)

Proof.

(i) We only prove the a priori estimate (
4.5
). By Duhamel’s formula, we have

\displaystyle u_{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}P^{\lambda}_{t-s}({H}_{s}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{s}+f_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s,

where

P^{\lambda}_{t}f(z)=\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda t}P_{t}f(z),\ \ z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2d},\ t>0.

By (
2.33
) with
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}={\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime}=\infty
, for any
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\alpha)
, we have

\displaystyle\|u_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda(t-s)}(t-s)^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\Big(\|{H}_{s}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}+\|f_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}\Big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}^{-\lambda(t-s)}(t-s)^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\Big(\|{H}_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}\|u_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{1+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}+\|f_{s}\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}\Big){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

(4.8)

In particular, taking
\beta^{\prime}=1
, by Hölder’s inequality, we have for
r=\frac{q}{q-1}
,

\displaystyle\|u_{t}\|^{r}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{1+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{H}\|^{r}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}^{-r\lambda(t-s)}(t-s)^{-\frac{r}{\alpha}}\|u_{s}\|^{r}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{1+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\|f\|^{r}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{e}^{-r\lambda s}s^{-\frac{r}{\alpha}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

Since
\frac{1}{\alpha}<\frac{1}{r}=1-\frac{1}{q}
, by Gronwall’s inequality, we get

\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{1+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\lambda^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{r}},\ \lambda\geqslant 1.

Let
1+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{q}
. By (
4.8
) and Young’s inequality we have for
\lambda\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q^{\prime}}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{1+\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\|H\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}+\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\right)\left(\int_{0}^{T}\mathrm{e}^{-r\lambda s}s^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}r}{\alpha}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/r}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\lambda^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{r}}\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{e}^{-rs}s^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}r}{\alpha}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/r}\lesssim\|f\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}.

Thus we get (
4.5
).

(ii) Let
U=u-\widetilde{u}
. Then
U
solves the following PDE:

\partial_{t}U=\Delta_{v}^{\alpha/2}U+v\cdot\nabla_{x}U-\lambda U+{H}\cdot\nabla_{v}U+G,\quad U_{0}=0,

where

G:=f-\widetilde{f}+({H}-\widetilde{H})\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{u}.

By (
4.5
), we have

\displaystyle\|U\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q^{\prime}}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\|G\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.9)

Note that

\displaystyle\|G\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|f-\widetilde{f}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}+\|(H-\widetilde{H})\cdot\nabla_{v}\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\|f-\widetilde{f}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}+\|H-\widetilde{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\|\widetilde{u}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{1+\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

Substituting it into (
4.9
) and by (
4.5
) with
q^{\prime}=\infty
and
\beta^{\prime}=1
, we obtain (
4.6
).

(iii) Noting that for any
j\geqslant 0
,

\|{\mathcal{R}}^{x}_{j}P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\|{\mathcal{R}}^{x}_{j}f\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

we have for any
\gamma\in{\mathbb{R}}
,

\|P_{t}f\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim(1\wedge t)^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

By this estimate, the estimate (
4.7
) is completely the same as (
4.5
).
∎

For
\alpha\in(1,2)
, let
N({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t)
be the Poisson random measure associated with
\alpha
-stable process
L^{(\alpha)}_{t}
.
More precisely, for any Borel set
E\in{\mathscr{B}}({\mathbb{R}}^{d})
and
t>0
,

N(E\times[0,t]):=\sum_{s\in(0,t]}{\mathbbm{1}}_{E}(L^{(\alpha)}_{s}-L^{(\alpha)}_{s-}).

Let
\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,[0,t]):=N({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,[0,t])-\nu^{(\alpha)}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w)t
be the compensated Poisson random measure,
where
\nu^{(\alpha)}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w)=c_{d,\alpha}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w/|w|^{d+\alpha}

is the Lévy measure of
\alpha
-stable process
L^{(\alpha)}_{t}
, and
c_{d,\alpha}
is a normalized constant only depending on
d,\alpha
.
In particular,

L^{(\alpha)}_{t}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}w\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,[0,t]).

The following lemma is crucial for weak uniqueness and pathwise uniqueness.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
T>0
,
\alpha\in(1,2]
,
\beta>0
and
q\in(\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},\infty]
. Suppose that
{H},f\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})
.
Let
u
be the unique weak solution of the following backward kinetic PDE (see Theorem
4.2
):

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+\Delta_{v}^{\alpha/2}u+v\cdot\nabla_{x}u+{H}\cdot\nabla_{v}u=f,\quad u(T)=0,

(4.10)

and
Z
be a solution of kinetic SDE (
4.2
). If
\alpha\in(1,2)
, then for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle u(t,Z_{t})=u(0,Z_{0})+\int^{t}_{0}f(s,Z_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{t}_{0}\!\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(u(s,Z_{s-}+\sigma w)-u(s,Z_{s-})\right)\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s);

(4.11)

if
\alpha=2
, then for any
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle u(t,Z_{t})=u(0,Z_{0})+\int^{t}_{0}f(s,Z_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{t}_{0}\nabla_{v}u(s,Z_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}W_{s}.

(4.12)

Proof.

We only prove (
4.11
) since (
4.12
) is similar and easier.
Let
\varGamma\in C^{\infty}_{c}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
be a smooth density function with support in
B^{\boldsymbol{a}}_{1}
.
For each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, define

\displaystyle\varGamma_{n}(z)=\varGamma_{n}(x,v):=n^{(2+\alpha)d}\varGamma(nx,n^{1+\alpha}v)

(4.13)

and

u^{n}(t,z):=(u(t,\cdot)*\varGamma_{n})(z).

Taking convolutions for both sides of (
4.10
) with
\varGamma_{n}
, we get

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{n}+(\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})u_{n}+{H}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{n}=f_{n}+g_{n}+h_{n},

where

\displaystyle g_{n}=v\cdot\nabla_{x}u_{n}-(v\cdot\nabla_{x}u)*\varGamma_{n},\ \ h_{n}:={H}\cdot\nabla_{v}u_{n}-({H}\cdot\nabla_{v}u)*\varGamma_{n}.

By Itô’s formula, we have

\displaystyle u_{n}(t,Z_{t})

\displaystyle=u_{n}(0,Z_{0})+\int^{t}_{0}(f_{n}+h_{n}+g_{n})(s,Z_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle+\int^{t}_{0}\!\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(u_{n}(s,Z_{s-}+\sigma w)-u_{n}(s,Z_{s-})\right)\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s).

Noting that by (
4.5
) with
q^{\prime}=\infty
and
\beta^{\prime}\in(0,\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{q})
,

\displaystyle\|u_{n}-u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{x,v}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}|u(t,x-y,v-w)-u(t,x,v)|\varGamma_{n}(y,w){\mathord{{\rm d}}}y{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w

\displaystyle\leqslant\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}(|y|^{\frac{1}{1+\alpha}}+|w|)\varGamma_{n}(y,w){\mathord{{\rm d}}}y{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w\lesssim n^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{1+\alpha}},

(4.14)

we have

\left(u_{n}(t,Z_{t}),u_{n}(0,Z_{0})\right)\to\left(u(t,Z_{t}),u(0,Z_{0})\right)\ \ a.s.,\ n\to\infty.

Let

A_{n}:=\int^{t}_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(u_{n}(s,Z_{s-}+\sigma w)-u_{n}(s,Z_{s-})\right)\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s)

and

A:=\int^{t}_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(u(s,Z_{s-}+\sigma w)-u(s,Z_{s-})\right)\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s).

Noting that

\displaystyle|(u_{n}(s,z+\sigma w)-u_{n}(s,z))-(u(s,z+\sigma w)-u(s,z))|

\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant\|u_{n}(s)-u(s)\|_{\infty}\wedge(\|\nabla_{v}u(s)\|_{\infty}|\sigma w|),

by the isometry formula of stochastic integrals, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}|A_{n}-A|^{2}\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}\!\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}\left(\|u_{n}(s)-u(s)\|_{\infty}^{2}\wedge\big(\|\nabla_{v}u(s)\|_{\infty}^{2}|\sigma w|^{2}\big)\right)\nu^{(\alpha)}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

Recalling
\nu^{(\alpha)}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w)=c_{d,\alpha}|w|^{-d-\alpha}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w
,
by (
4.14
) and the dominated convergence theorem, we get

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}{\mathbb{E}}|A_{n}-A|^{2}=0.

Finally, noting that

\displaystyle g_{n}(t,z)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}u(t,x-y,v-w)w\cdot\nabla_{y}\varGamma_{n}(y,w){\mathord{{\rm d}}}y{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,

since
\varGamma_{n}(y,w)=n^{(2+\alpha)d}\varGamma(ny,n^{1+\alpha}w)
, we have

\displaystyle\|g_{n}(t)\|_{\infty}\lesssim n^{-\alpha}\|u(t)\|_{\infty}

and

\displaystyle\|h_{n}(t)\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim n^{-\beta/(1+\alpha)}\|{H}_{t}\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}\|\nabla_{v}u(t)\|_{\infty}.

Hence, by (
4.5
) with
(q^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})=(\infty,1)
and the dominated convergence theorem,

\int^{t}_{0}(|f_{n}-f|+|h_{n}|+|g_{n}|)(s,Z_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\to 0,\ \ a.s.,\ n\to\infty.

Thus we complete the proof of (
4.11
).
∎

Remark 4.4
.

The stochastic integrals in (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) are martingales by the regularity estimates of
u
in (
4.5
).

Now, we can give the

Proof of Theorem
4.1
:

(
Existence of weak solutions
) Since
{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})
is continuous in the spatial variable,
the existence of a weak solution is standard by weak convergence method. We referred to
[
35
]
and
[
28
]
.

(
Weak uniqueness
) Let
Z^{1}
and
Z^{2}
be two weak solutions of SDE (
4.2
) with the same initial distribution.
For any
f\in{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}
, by Lemma
4.3
with
T=t
, we have

{\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}f(Z^{1}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s=-{\mathbb{E}}u(0,Z^{1}_{0})=-{\mathbb{E}}u(0,Z^{2}_{0})={\mathbb{E}}\int_{0}^{t}f(Z^{2}_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

Hence,

{\mathbb{E}}f(Z^{1}_{t})={\mathbb{E}}f(Z^{2}_{t}).

In particular,
Z^{1}
and
Z^{2}
have the same one dimensional marginal distribution. Thus,
as in
[
35
, Theorem 4.4.3]
, by a standard method,
we obtain the uniqueness.

(
Strong uniqueness and stability
)
We use the well-known Zvonkin transformation to prove the strong stability, which automatically imply the pathwise uniqueness.
The strong uniqueness then follows by Yamada-Watanabe’s theorem. Below we only prove it for
\alpha\in(1,2)
.
Recall that

{H},\widetilde{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\frac{\gamma}{1+\alpha}}_{x}\cap{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}),\ \ \beta>1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2},\ \ \gamma=\alpha+\beta.

By the interpolation inequality (
2.20
), we have for any
\theta\in[0,1]
,

{H},\widetilde{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\theta\gamma,(1-\theta)\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}).

Fix
T>0
and
\lambda\geqslant 1
. Let
{\mathbf{u}}:=(u_{1},\cdots,u_{d})
solve the following backward PDE:

\partial_{t}{\mathbf{u}}+(\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}+v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\lambda){\mathbf{u}}+{H}\cdot\nabla_{v}{\mathbf{u}}+{H}=0,\quad{\mathbf{u}}(T)=0.

By (
4.7
), we have for any
\beta^{\prime}\in[0,\alpha+\frac{\alpha}{q^{\prime}}-\frac{\alpha}{q})
and
\theta\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q^{\prime}}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\theta\gamma,\beta^{\prime}+(1-\theta)\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\|{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\theta\gamma,(1-\theta)\beta}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.15)

Similarly, we define
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}
by
\widetilde{H}
. Then (
4.15
) also holds for
\widetilde{H}
and
\widetilde{u}
.
Moreover, by (
4.6
) we have

\displaystyle\|{\mathbf{u}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q^{\prime}}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1-\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}\|H-\widetilde{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}.

(4.16)

In particular, letting
q^{\prime}=\infty
,
\theta=1
in (
4.15
) and
\beta^{\prime}\in(1,\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{q})
,
by (
2.18
), we obtain

\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}}\vee\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}}\lesssim\|{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta^{\prime}}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\vee\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma,\beta^{\prime}}_{x,{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\lambda^{\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}-1},

where the implicit constant is independent of
\lambda\geqslant 1
.
Since
\frac{1}{q}+\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\alpha}<1
, one can choose
\lambda
large enough so that

\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}}\vee\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}}\leqslant\tfrac{1}{2}.

Now if we define

\Phi(t,z)=\Phi(t,x,v):=(x,v+{\mathbf{u}}(t,x,v)),

then for
z=(x,v)
and
z^{\prime}=(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})
,

|z-z^{\prime}|\leqslant|\Phi(t,z)-\Phi(t,z^{\prime})|+|{\mathbf{u}}(t,z)-{\mathbf{u}}(t,z^{\prime})|\leqslant|\Phi(t,z)-\Phi(t,z^{\prime})|+\tfrac{1}{2}|z-z^{\prime}|,

which implies

\displaystyle|z-z^{\prime}|\leqslant 2|\Phi(t,z)-\Phi(t,z^{\prime})|.

(4.17)

Similarly, we define
\widetilde{\Phi}
in terms of
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}
.
On the other hand, since
\beta>1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}
, by (
2.18
) and (
4.15
) with
q^{\prime}=q
and
\theta=0,1
,
it is easy to see that for some
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,

\displaystyle\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\alpha/2+\varepsilon_{0}}_{v})}\vee\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\alpha/2+\varepsilon_{0}}_{v})}<\infty.

(4.18)

By (
4.11
) with
f=-{H}
, one finds that

\displaystyle\Phi(t,Z_{t})=\Phi(0,Z_{0})+\int^{t}_{0}(V_{s},0){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{t}_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}g(s,Z_{s-},w)\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s),

(4.19)

where

g(s,z,w):=\Phi(s,z+\sigma w)-\Phi(s,z)=(0,w+{\mathbf{u}}(s,z+\sigma w)-{\mathbf{u}}(s,z)).

Similarly, we define

\widetilde{g}(s,z,w):=\widetilde{\Phi}(s,z+\sigma w)-\widetilde{\Phi}(s,z)=(0,w+\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s,z+\sigma w)-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s,z)).

Let
\widetilde{Z}
be any solution of SDE (
4.12
) corresponding to
\widetilde{H}
and initial value
\widetilde{Z}_{0}
. Define

U_{t}:=Z_{t}-\widetilde{Z}_{t},\ \ \Lambda_{t}:=\Phi(t,Z_{t})-\widetilde{\Phi}(t,\widetilde{Z}_{t}),\ G_{s}(w):=g(s,Z_{s-},w)-\widetilde{g}(s,\widetilde{Z}_{s-},w).

By (
4.19
), we have

\Lambda_{t}=\Lambda_{0}+\int^{t}_{0}(V_{s}-\widetilde{V}_{s},0){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+\int^{t}_{0}\!\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}G_{s}(w)\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s).

By BDG’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|\Lambda_{s}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}|\Lambda_{0}|^{2}+\int^{t}_{0}{\mathbb{E}}|V_{s}-\widetilde{V}_{s}|^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sup_{s^{\prime}\in[0,t]}\left|\int^{s^{\prime}}_{0}\!\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}G_{s}(w)\widetilde{N}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s)\right|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}|\Lambda_{0}|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}{\mathbb{E}}|U_{s}|^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s+{\mathbb{E}}\left(\int^{t}_{0}\!\!\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}|G_{s}(w)|^{2}\nu^{(\alpha)}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right).

(4.20)

Noting that

\displaystyle g(s,z,w)-g(s,\widetilde{z},w)=(z-\widetilde{z})\cdot\int_{0}^{1}\nabla_{z}g(s,\theta z+(1-\theta)\widetilde{z},w){\mathord{{\rm d}}}\theta,

and

\nabla_{z}g(s,z,w)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0,&\nabla_{x}{\mathbf{u}}(s,z+\sigma w)-\nabla_{x}{\mathbf{u}}(s,z)\\
0,&\nabla_{v}{\mathbf{u}}(s,z+\sigma w)-\nabla_{v}{\mathbf{u}}(s,z)\end{array}\right),

by (
4.18
), we have for
\delta=\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon_{0}
,

|g(s,z,w)-g(s,\widetilde{z},w)|\lesssim|z-\widetilde{z}|\left(\left(\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\delta}_{v}}|w|^{\delta}\right)\wedge\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{\infty}\right).

Similarly, we have

|g(s,z,w)-\widetilde{g}(s,z,w)|\lesssim\left(\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\delta}_{v}}|w|^{\delta}\right)\wedge\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{\infty}.

Hence,

\displaystyle|G_{s}(w)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|U_{s}|\left(\left(\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\delta}_{v}}|w|^{\delta}\right)\wedge\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\delta}_{v}}|w|^{\delta}\right)\wedge\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{\infty}.

Recalling
\nu^{(\alpha)}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w)=c_{d,\alpha}|w|^{-d-\alpha}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}w
, for
\delta>\frac{\alpha}{2}
and any
a,b>0
, we have

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}(a|w|^{\delta}\wedge b)^{2}\nu^{(\alpha)}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}w)\leqslant C(a^{2}+b^{2}).

Moreover, by (
4.17
) we also have

|U_{s}|\leqslant 2|\Lambda_{s}|+2\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|_{\infty}.

Combining the above calculations and by (
4.20
), we get for all
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,t]}|U_{s}|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}|U_{0}|^{2}+\|{\mathbf{u}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}}+\int^{T}_{0}\|{\mathbf{u}}(s)-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\delta}_{v}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s

\displaystyle\quad+\int^{t}_{0}\Big(1+\|\nabla{\mathbf{u}}(s)\|^{2}_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\delta}_{v}}\Big){\mathbb{E}}|U_{s}|^{2}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

By Gronwall’s inequality and (
4.18
), we obtain

{\mathbb{E}}\left(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|U_{s}|^{2}\right)\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}|U_{0}|^{2}+\|{\mathbf{u}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{T}}+\|{\mathbf{u}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{L}}^{2}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\delta}_{v})},

which in turn implies (
4.3
) by (
4.16
).
∎

4.2.
Proofs of Theorems
1.3
and
1.9

Suppose that (
1.22
) and (
1.23
) hold. Let

\displaystyle\gamma_{b}>({\mathscr{Q}}-1-{\mathscr{A}}_{1})\vee 0={\mathscr{Q}}-1-({\mathscr{Q}}-1)\wedge{\mathscr{A}}_{1}.

(4.21)

Recall the parameter set
\Theta
defined in (
3.7
). Under (
1.22
) and (
1.23
),
by Theorem
3.11
, there is a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\Theta)>0
such that
for any fixed
T\geqslant 1
and
\mu_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}\cap{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})
with

\displaystyle\|\mu_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\leqslant C_{0}T^{-\gamma_{b}/\alpha}/\kappa_{b},

(4.22)

where
\kappa_{b}
is from (
1.23
),
there is a unique smooth solution
u_{T}
to FPE (
1.5
) with
b_{T}=b{\mathbbm{1}}_{[0,T]}
and initial value
u_{T}(0)=\mu_{0}

so that for any
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\|u_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}}\leqslant C_{\beta},

where
{\mathbb{S}}_{\beta}
is defined by (
3.21
).
In the following we fix time
T\geqslant 1
and drop the subscript
T
in
u_{T}
.

Recalling the notations in Section
3
, we first show the following
L^{1}
-estimate.

Lemma 4.5
.

For any
t\in[0,T]
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\leqslant 1.

(4.23)

Proof.

We use the stability in Theorem
3.11
to show (
4.23
).
Let
b^{n}:=b*\varGamma_{n}
and
u^{n}_{0}:=\mu_{0}*\varGamma_{n}
be the modifying approximation of
b
and
\mu_{0}
,
where
\varGamma_{n}
is defined in (
4.13
). By definitions, it is easy to see that

b^{n}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}(C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})),\ \ u^{n}_{0}\in C^{\infty}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d})\cap L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}),

and

\sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|b^{n}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}\leqslant\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})},\ \ \sup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\|u^{n}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}\leqslant\|u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}},

and for any
\varepsilon>0
,

\displaystyle\|b^{n}-b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{\varepsilon}^{\prime})}+\|u^{n}_{0}-u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}-\varepsilon}}\to 0\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

(4.24)

Let
u^{n}\in C([0,T];C^{2}_{b}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}))
be the smooth solution to the following kinetic Fokker-Planck equation

\partial_{t}u^{n}=(\Delta^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}_{v}-v\cdot\nabla_{x})u^{n}-\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}((b^{n}*u^{n})u^{n}),\ u^{n}(0)=u^{n}_{0}.

By Lemma
C.1
in appendix, we have

\displaystyle\|u^{n}(t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}\leqslant\|u^{n}(0)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}=\|\mu_{0}*\varGamma_{n}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{1}}=1.

(4.25)

By (
1.22
), one can fix
\varepsilon>0
being small enough so that

\displaystyle{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-(\beta_{0}-\varepsilon)-(\beta_{b}-\varepsilon)<{\mathscr{Q}}+(\beta_{0}-\varepsilon)\wedge(-1).

Using
(\beta_{0}-\varepsilon,\beta_{b}-\varepsilon)
to replace
(\beta_{0},\beta_{b})
in Theorem
3.11

and slightly adjusting the smallness constant in (
4.22
), by (
4.24
) and the stability estimate (
3.32
),
for each
t\in(0,T]
, one has

\lim_{n\to\infty}\|u^{n}(t)-u(t)\|_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}}=0.

Thus by (
4.25
) and Fatou’s lemma, we conclude (
4.23
).
∎

The following lemma improves the regularity estimate in (
3.31
).

Lemma 4.6
.

For any
\beta>\beta_{0}
, there is a constant
C=C(\Theta,\beta,T,\kappa_{b},\|\mu_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}})>0
such that

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant Ct^{-\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}},\ t\in(0,T].

(4.26)

Proof.

For
\theta={\mathscr{A}}_{0}-2\beta_{0}-\beta_{b},

by (
1.22
) and (
4.21
), we have

\theta\in(0,{\mathscr{Q}}),\ \ \beta_{0}\leqslant{\mathscr{Q}}-1-\theta,\ \ 0\,\hbox to4.4pt{\vbox to7.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.2pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 9.69 L 5.53 6.92 M 0 8.3 L 5.53 5.53 L 0 2.77 L 5.53 0 L 5.53 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\,{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}+{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}-(\theta,{\mathscr{Q}}-1).

Thus for
i=0,1
, by (
3.14
) with
({\boldsymbol{p}}^{\prime},{\boldsymbol{p}})=({\boldsymbol{p}}_{i},{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i})
,

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{P2}}}{{\lesssim}}\|{\mathcal{H}}^{b}(u,u)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{B}}^{{\mathscr{Q}}-1-\theta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|b_{T}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{b}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\ell}}_{0}}({\mathbb{X}}_{0})}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0}}({\mathbb{L}}^{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i}})}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AB2}}}{{\lesssim}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}T^{\gamma_{b}/\alpha}\|u\|_{{\mathbb{S}}_{0}}^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AS6}}}{{\lesssim}}\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}T^{\gamma_{b}/\alpha}\|\mu_{0}\|^{2}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}.

On the other hand, by Lemma
2.16
we have

\|P_{\cdot}u_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{\infty}_{0}({\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|\mu_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},\ i=0,1.

Combining the above two estimates, we obtain

\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant C,\ t\in(0,T],

For general case
\beta>\beta_{0}
, it follows by (
3.30
)
and the real interpolation theorem (see
[
8
, Theorem 6.4.5, (1)]
).
∎

Define

\displaystyle{H}(s,z):=(b(s)*u(s))(z).

(4.27)

To use Theorem
4.1
, the following lemma provides necessary estimates of
{H}
for linear SDE.

Lemma 4.7
.

(i) Under (
1.22
) and (
1.23
), there are
q>\tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
and
\beta>0
such that

{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}).

(ii) Under (
1.25
) and (
1.26
), there are
q>\tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
and
\beta>1-\tfrac{\alpha}{2}
such that

{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma}_{x}\cap{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}),\ \ \gamma:=\tfrac{\beta+\alpha}{1+\alpha}.

(iii) Let
\widetilde{b}
be another kernel satisfying (
1.23
) and (
1.26
), and
\widetilde{\mu}_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}
satisfy (
4.22
).
Let
\widetilde{u}
be the
unique smooth solution of FPE (
1.5
) corresponding to
\widetilde{b}
and
\widetilde{u}_{0}
. Define
\widetilde{H}_{s}(z):=(\widetilde{b}(s)*\widetilde{u}(s))(z)
. Then
there are
q>\tfrac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
,
\beta>0
and constant
C=C(T,\Theta,q,\beta)>0
such that

\|{H}-\widetilde{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim_{C}\|\mu_{0}-\widetilde{\mu}_{0}\|_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{\beta_{0}}}+\|b-\widetilde{b}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}_{0}^{\prime})}.

Proof.

For any
\beta\geqslant 0
, since
{\mathscr{A}}_{1}\leqslant{\mathscr{A}}_{0}
, we have for any
t\in(0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}=\sum_{i=0,1}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{Sob}}}{{\lesssim}}\sum_{i=0,1}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b}+{\mathscr{A}}_{i},1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{AZ3}}}{{\lesssim}}t^{-\frac{\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}.

(4.28)

(i) Recall
b=b_{0}+b_{1}
. For
\beta\geqslant 0
, by (
2.13
) we have

\|b(t)*u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}}}=\|b(t)*u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\Big(\|b_{0}(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\|b_{1}(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\Big)\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}=\|b(t)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbb{X}}_{\beta}}.

Since
0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{q_{b}}-1
and
q_{b}>\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
,
one can choose
q\in(\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1},q_{b})
and
\beta>0
so that

0<\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<\tfrac{\alpha}{q}-\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}}=:\tfrac{\alpha}{r}.

Thus by Hölder’s inequality and (
4.28
), we have

\|H\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}=\|b*u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{\boldsymbol{a}})}\lesssim\|b\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{0}({\mathbb{X}}^{\prime}_{0})}\left(\int^{T}_{0}t^{-r(\beta+\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}+{\mathscr{A}}_{0})/\alpha}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t\right)^{1/r}<\infty.

(ii) Since
0<{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<\tfrac{3}{2}\alpha-\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}}-2
,
one can choose
q>\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
and
\beta>1-\frac{\alpha}{2}
so that

0<\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}<\tfrac{\alpha}{q}-\tfrac{\alpha}{q_{b}}=:\tfrac{\alpha}{r}.

Let
\delta=\frac{\alpha}{2}-1
. Fix
i=0,1
. By (
2.13
), we have

\|b_{i}(t)*u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\|b_{i}(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}},

and for all
j\geqslant 0
,

\|{\mathcal{R}}^{x}_{j}b_{i}(t)*u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\delta+\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\|{\mathcal{R}}^{x}_{j}b_{i}(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\delta+\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\|u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta-\beta_{b},1}_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\varrho}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}.

In particular, by Definition
2.2
and (
4.28
), for
i=0,1
,

\displaystyle\|b_{i}(t)*u(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha-\delta,\delta+\beta}_{\infty;x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\lesssim\|b_{i}(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha-\delta,\delta+\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}t^{-\frac{\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b}}{\alpha}}.

By Hölder’s inequality, we get

\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha-\delta,\delta+\beta}_{\infty;x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|b_{i}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q_{b}}_{T}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha-\delta,\delta+\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i};{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\left(\int^{T}_{0}t^{-\frac{r(\beta+{\mathscr{A}}_{0}-\beta_{0}-\beta_{b})\vee 0}{\alpha}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}t\right)^{1/r}<\infty.

Since
\delta+\beta>0
, by (
2.19
), we have for
\gamma=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{1+\alpha}
,

\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\gamma}_{x}\cap{\mathbf{C}}^{\beta}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}})}=\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{B}}^{\gamma}_{\infty;x}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}\lesssim\|b_{i}*u\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha-\delta,\delta+\beta}_{\infty;x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty;{\boldsymbol{a}}})}<\infty.

(iii) It follows by the calculations in (i) and (
3.32
).
∎

Remark 4.8
.

In the situation of Remark
3.6
, by Remark
3.13
, the above (i) still holds.

Now we are in a position to give

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Let
\alpha\in(1,2]
. Consider the following linear SDE:

{\mathord{{\rm d}}}Z_{t}={\mathbf{H}}_{t}(Z_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+\sigma{\mathord{{\rm d}}}L^{(\alpha)}_{t},\ \ Z_{0}\sim\mu_{0},

where
{\mathbf{H}}_{t}(x,v)=(v,H_{t}(x,v))
is defined in (
4.27
).
By Lemma
4.7
and Theorem
4.1
, there is a unique weak/strong solution to the above SDE
under (
1.22
)-(
1.23
) and (
1.25
)-(
1.26
), respectively.
It remains to show that

\displaystyle\mu_{t}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z):=\mbox{Law of }Z_{t}=u(t,z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z.

(4.29)

Let

w(t,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z):=\mu_{t}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)-u(t,z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z\in{\mathcal{M}}({\mathbb{R}}^{2d}).

Clearly, by (
4.23
) one has

\|w(t)\|_{\mathcal{M}}=\sup_{\|f\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1}\left|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}f(z)w(t,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z)\right|\leqslant 2,

and
w
solves the following FPE with initial value
w(0)=0
in the distributional sense:

\partial_{t}w=(\Delta^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}_{v}-v\cdot\nabla_{x})w-\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}({H}w).

By Duhamel’s formula, we have

w(t,z)+\int^{t}_{0}P_{t-s}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}({H}_{s}w_{s}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s=0,\ t\in(0,T].

Let
P_{t}^{*}
be the adjoint semigroup of
P_{t}
(see (
2.22
)).
By the definition of total variation norm,

\displaystyle\|w(t)\|_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|f\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1}\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}P_{t-s}\mathord{{\rm div}}_{v}({H}_{s}w_{s})(z)f(z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}z{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right|

\displaystyle=\sup_{\|f\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1}\left|\int^{t}_{0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}}{H}_{s}(z)\cdot\nabla_{v}P^{*}_{t-s}f(z)w(s,{\mathord{{\rm d}}}z){\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{\|f\|_{\infty}\leqslant 1}\int^{t}_{0}\|{H}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|\nabla_{v}P^{*}_{t-s}f\|_{\infty}\|w(s)\|_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s.

Let
q>\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
be as in (i) of Lemma
4.7
and
\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{q}=1
. Since

\|\nabla_{v}P^{*}_{t-s}f\|_{\infty}\lesssim(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}\|f\|_{\infty}
(see Lemma
2.10
), by Hölder’s inequality we
further have

\displaystyle\|w(t)\|_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int^{t}_{0}(t-s)^{-\frac{1}{\alpha}}\|{H}_{s}\|_{\infty}\|w(s)\|_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\ \leqslant\|{H}\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbb{L}}^{\infty})}\left(\int^{t}_{0}(t-s)^{-\frac{r}{\alpha}}\|w(s)\|^{r}_{\mathcal{M}}{\mathord{{\rm d}}}s\right)^{1/r},

which, by
\frac{r}{\alpha}<1
and Gronwall’s inequality, yields
w(t)=0
. Thus we obtain (
4.29
).

(i) The smoothness of density and short time singularity estimates follow by (
4.26
).

(ii) The large time decay estimate follows by Theorem
3.14
.

(iii) The stability of the density follows by (
3.32
).

(iv) The strong stability follows by Theorem
4.1
and (iii) of Lemma
4.7
.

(v) The global existence follows by Theorems
3.19
,
3.20
and Remark
3.18
.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

Under the assumptions of Remark
3.6
, by Remark
4.8
, it is easy to see that the weak well-posedness still holds.

Next we give the

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

We only show the two-sides estimates of the density since the others are completely the same as Theorem
1.3
.
Let
u
be the unique solution of (
3.60
) and define
H_{t}(x)=b(t)*u(t)(x)
.
Consider the following linear SDE:

{\mathord{{\rm d}}}X_{t}=H_{t}(X_{t}){\mathord{{\rm d}}}t+{\mathord{{\rm d}}}L^{(\alpha)}_{t}.

Since
{H}\in{\mathbb{L}}^{q}_{T}({\mathbf{C}}^{\beta})
for some
q>\frac{\alpha}{\alpha-1}
and
\beta>0
,
it is well known that for each starting point
X_{0}=x
,
X_{t}
has a density
p(t,x,y)
enjoying
two-sides estimates: for
\alpha\in(1,2)
(see
[
26
]
),

p(t,x,y)\asymp t(t^{1/\alpha}+|x-y|)^{-d-\alpha},

and for
\alpha=2
(see
[
26
, Theorem 1.1]
),

C^{-1}_{1}t^{-d/2}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{|x-y|^{2}}{C_{2}t}}\leqslant p(t,x,y)\leqslant C_{1}t^{-d/2}\mathrm{e}^{-\frac{C_{2}|x-y|^{2}}{t}}.

For general starting point
X_{0}\sim\mu_{0}
, we have

\rho(t,y)=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}p(t,x,y)\mu_{0}({\mathord{{\rm d}}}x).

The desired estimates now follows.
∎

5.
Application to fractional Vlasov-Poisson-Fokker-Planck equation

Let
d\geqslant 3
.
In this section, we apply Theorem
3.14
to the following VPFP equation in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}
:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f=\Delta^{\alpha/2}_{v}f-v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\gamma\nabla U\cdot\nabla_{v}f,

(5.1)

where
\alpha\in(1,2]
and
\gamma=\pm 1
stands for the attractive or repulsive force in physics, respectively,

\displaystyle U(t,x)=(|\cdot|^{2-d}*\langle f\rangle(t))(x),\quad\langle f\rangle(t,x):=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{d}}f(t,x,v){\mathord{{\rm d}}}v.

Let

K(x):=\nabla|\cdot|^{2-d}(x),\ \ \widetilde{K}(x,v):=K(x).

With these notations, we have

E(x):=\nabla U*\langle f\rangle(x)=K*\langle f\rangle(x)=(\widetilde{K}*f)(x,v).

By Lemma
A.3
and Proposition
A.4
, we have

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
\varrho_{0},\varrho_{1}\in[1,\infty]
and
\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{i}:=(\varrho_{i},\infty)
,
i=0,1
.
If either
\varrho_{0}>\frac{d}{d-1}
and
\varrho_{1}\in[1,\infty]
or

\varrho_{0}<\frac{d}{d-1}
and
\varrho_{1}\in(\tfrac{d}{d-1},\infty]
,
then for any
\beta_{b}\leqslant(1+\alpha)(\tfrac{d}{\varrho_{0}}+1-d)
,

\widetilde{K}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}+{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{b}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}.

Moreover, for
s_{0}=1+\frac{\alpha}{2}
and
s_{1}=\frac{\alpha}{2}-1+\beta_{b}
,

\widetilde{K}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{s_{0},s_{1}}_{\boldsymbol{\varrho}_{0};x,{\boldsymbol{a}}}.

As an application of Theorem
3.14
, we have

Theorem 5.2
.

Let
\alpha\in(1,2]
,
p_{0}\in[2,\infty]
,
{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0}:=(p_{0},1)
and
\beta_{0}\in(-\alpha,0)
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\tfrac{(1+\alpha)d}{p_{0}}=2\alpha+\beta_{0},\ \beta_{0}\in(-1,0),\ \ \tfrac{(1+\alpha)d}{p_{0}}<2\alpha+1+2\beta_{0},\ \beta_{0}\in(-\alpha,-1].

(5.2)

For any
f_{0}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}\cap{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}
,
there is a constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\alpha,\beta_{0},p_{0},d,\|f_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}})>0
such that if

\displaystyle\|f_{0}\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta_{0}}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\leqslant C_{0},

(5.3)

then there is a unique global smooth solution
f
to VPFP (
5.1
) such that for any
\beta\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(0,1]}\left(t^{\frac{\beta-\beta_{0}}{\alpha}}\big(\|f(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{{\boldsymbol{p}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}+\|f(t)\|_{{\mathbf{B}}^{\beta,1}_{\boldsymbol{1};{\boldsymbol{a}}}}\big)\right)<\infty,

(5.4)

and for any
